**Cycle 91 (2026-07-17) — the most-stale row this project's own staleness-check methodology has caught yet, found and corrected with the same rigor in both directions.** Cycle 88 established a pattern — re-verify a frozen scorecard row's claims directly against the current repo rather than trusting old cycle numbers — and closed it out flagging three sibling rows under "AI & clinical depth" as candidates for the same treatment: `OCR & smart intake`, `EMS & triage workflows`, `Clinical tool breadth vs. depth`. All three were checked this cycle. Two held up exactly as recorded: OCR & smart intake's "still WARN" claims (`ENABLE_MONGOOSE_EMERGENCY_OS` still gated off by default; Tesseract still the live default provider with real-world handwriting accuracy still unmeasured) were re-verified true today by reading `environment.config.ts` and `ocr-providers.ts` directly, and Clinical tool breadth's own number was re-counted against `REGISTERED_EXECUTOR_TOOL_IDS` in `tool-orchestrator.registry.ts` — still exactly 39, matching the record precisely. Neither moved, correctly, since neither was actually stale. **`EMS & triage workflows` was a different story — frozen since roughly Cycle 47-50, about 40 cycles, the longest gap this methodology has caught.** In that gap, real capability shipped and was never credited to this row: `EMSPipeline.tsx` grew a materially richer sub-component set (offload tracking, ambulance handoff checklists, pre-arrival notification forms, resource activation) that didn't exist at the row's last check; a real `@Post('ems/handoff')` backend route now exists, delegating to a real service — verified by reading the handler directly, with an honest caveat surfaced in the same pass rather than glossed over: its persistence (`WorkflowActionLogService`) is an in-memory array capped at 500 entries, not a TypeORM entity, so completion survives a browser refresh and is visible across workstations hitting the same running server, but not a restart — the exact class of gap Cycle 77 closed for AI action proposals, not yet closed here. A full, separate journey-engine layer was also confirmed real: `src/engine/journeyEngine.ts`, an 11-state FSM (Arrival→Registration→Triage→Waiting→Assessment→Orders/Disposition→Results→Admission/Discharge) with real `VALID_TRANSITIONS` enforcement and a dedicated `PatientJourneyTransitionError`; a mounted, routed `FullJourneyOperatingPage.tsx` (1,156 lines, confirmed wired into `router.tsx`, not orphaned); real `dispatcher`/`ems_coordinator` RBAC roles; and 3 dedicated orchestration services (`emergencyCareJourneyOrchestrator.ts`, `fullEmergencyCareJourneyService.ts`, `patientJourneyAiDecisionService.ts`). None of this was cited from memory — **freshly run this cycle: 32 tests across 7 files, all green** (5 files/15 tests for the journey/dispatch services, `emergencyStore.workflowActions.test.ts` 15/15 for journeyEngine's real integration coverage via the store, `selfArrivalTriageEngine.test.ts` 2/2). The already-fresh-verified Interactive-Intelligence EMS browser suite (Cycle 88, 8/8) was EMS-relevant capability this specific row had also never credited. **EMS & triage workflows WARN 50→63; Clinical workflow 81→85** — held well short of the sub-row's own +13 move, since the other two AI & clinical depth rows were checked but didn't move, and shouldn't ride along on one row's evidence. **Lesson reinforced, not new: a score frozen for many cycles is exactly as suspect as a score that just moved — the fix is always to re-verify the specific claims, not to assume either direction.**

**Cycle 90 (2026-07-17) — closed a real diagnostic-tooling gap Cycle 87 flagged as needed, used it rigorously, and got a real answer to one question plus a sharper, still-open version of another.** Cycle 87 left the `calculator-bmi` TBT investigation stuck on a tooling limit: `scripts/perf-profile.mjs` only wraps CDP `Profiler.stop()` — pure V8 sampling — so any main-thread cost it can't attribute to a JS stack frame, including actual rendering work (Layout, Recalculate Style, Paint) and pure idle/wait gaps, all falls into one undifferentiated `(program)` bucket. There was no way to tell "expensive render" apart from "expensive script" apart from "just waiting." New `scripts/perf-trace.mjs` uses CDP `Tracing.start()`/`Tracing.end()` instead, capturing real category-labeled trace events rather than statistical samples. Getting it right took catching two real methodology bugs before trusting a single number from it, not one clean pass: (1) the first draft summed every Chrome process's events indiscriminately — the GPU process, V8's own background compile/parse worker threads — and 87% of total time landed in "Other," traced to background streaming-parse/compile threads that by design never block the main thread, so summing them in wildly overstates real cost; fixed by isolating events to the `CrRendererMain` thread via Chrome's own `thread_name` metadata events. (2) Complete trace events nest (`RunTask` contains `FunctionCall` contains `EvaluateScript` contains `Layout`), so summing gross durations double-counts every level of nesting; replaced with a proper stack-based self-time computation — duration minus time spent in children — the same technique a flame chart's own bottom-up view uses. With a correctly-scoped tool, re-profiled `calculator-bmi` against `calculators-hub` (2 runs each, stable): **main-thread self-time is essentially equal between the two routes** — 760–935ms for `calculator-bmi`, 911–1001ms for `calculators-hub` — and in both, Script dominates (~52%) while Layout+Style are a modest ~15–20% combined and Paint is under 1%. That's a real, definitive answer to Cycle 87's open question: **the `calculator-bmi` outlier is not caused by expensive Layout/Style/Paint rendering work** — for both routes alike, script execution is the dominant main-thread cost, not rendering. This cycle didn't stop at that comfortable answer, though — cross-checked it against a fresh, port-verified, 3-run live re-measurement using the project's own established TBT harness (`e2e/performance.spec.mjs`, matching Cycles 86/87's exact isolated-`vite preview` methodology) to confirm the underlying finding is still current, not stale: it is. `calculator-bmi` TBT is a stable 1,070–1,098ms across 3 fresh runs today; `calculators-hub` is 277–342ms — the same ~3x gap Cycle 86 found, real and reproducible right now, unresolved by any intervening cycle's work. That produces a genuinely new, sharper, still-open puzzle rather than a closed one: **if total main-thread self-time is roughly equal between the two routes, the TBT gap can't be about how much work happens — it has to be about how that work is shaped into tasks.** TBT specifically penalizes a few long uninterrupted blocking tasks over the same total work spread across many short ones; the browser's own `longtask` API confirmed a similar task *count* for both routes (4–5 for `calculator-bmi`, 5–6 for `calculators-hub`), so the mechanism isn't obviously "far more tasks" either — the precise cause of the per-task duration gap is genuinely open, and reported as such rather than force-fit to whichever theory the data half-supports. `scripts/perf-trace.mjs` is real, reusable, and `eslint` clean. **No gauge moves this cycle** — consistent with the exact discipline Cycle 87 itself established: real, rigorous investigative work that narrows a hypothesis without moving the metric the whole thread is about doesn't earn score credit just because engineering happened. Runtime performance's own row is updated to reflect the sharper, current understanding (Layout/Paint ruled out; live re-measurement reconfirmed as current; new tool documented) without moving its score.

**Cycle 89 (2026-07-17) — closed a real, named roadmap item instead of finding another stale row.** Cycle 77's IX16 closure (write-through TypeORM journal for AI action proposals) shipped with an explicit "honest remainder" in its own entry: "the pre-consolidation design's hash-chain audit per transition not yet re-added." That remainder sat untouched for 12 cycles. This cycle re-added it, for real: a new `ai_action_proposal_audit_entries` table stores one append-only row per state transition — including the initial creation and lazy auto-expiry, not just the explicit approve/reject/execute/rollback calls — and every row's `entryHash` is a sha256 digest covering its own fields (proposal id, sequence index, from/to state, actor, timestamp, metadata) *plus* the immediately preceding row's `entryHash`. That chaining is the entire point: change a single stored field on any row, or delete one from the middle of the sequence, and every hash from that point forward stops matching what a fresh recomputation produces — there's no way to edit history without it being detectable, and no separate signing key or external system is needed to prove it. `verifyAuditChain()` does that recomputation using the *exact same* pure hash function `appendAuditEntry()` uses to write entries in the first place (a verifier that recomputed hashes with even slightly different logic than the writer would be worse than useless — it would let real tampering through). Proven with an actual tamper scenario, not just unit-tested happy paths: create a proposal, approve it, transition it to executing, let it write through to a mocked durable log, then directly corrupt one field (`actorUserId`) on the stored row for the approval step — exactly what an attacker with database write access but no way to recompute three entries' worth of downstream hashes could do. Restart the service (a fresh instance, matching the IX16 restart-survival pattern exactly), rehydrate from the now-tampered log, and `verifyAuditChain()` correctly reports `{ valid: false, brokenAtSequenceIndex: 1 }` — pinpointing the exact transition that was altered, not just "something's wrong somewhere." A companion test confirms the opposite case is equally solid: an untampered chain, rehydrated after a real restart, is byte-for-byte identical to the live in-memory version and verifies clean. New `GET /api/ai/proposals/:proposalId/audit` route exposes both the trail and the verification result. The migration was verified the way every migration in this project's history has been — a full forward-from-empty, revert, forward-again round trip against a real SQLite database, not just "it compiled": chain now **31/31 from empty**, zero errors either direction. 9 new tests plus the full pre-existing `ai` module suite re-run clean, **55/55**, confirming nothing about proposal creation, approval, execution, or the existing journal broke. `tsc`/eslint 0/0 on every touched and newly-created file — including auto-fixing 11 pre-existing prettier violations in `ai.controller.ts` that predated this change but were sitting in a file this cycle was already touching. **Backend reliability & ops GOOD 80→82; Backend reliability 82→83.** Left alone, correctly: the *other* half of Cycle 77's remainder — single-node vs. multi-instance read-model consistency for the in-process proposal store — is a distinct, larger architectural question (horizontal scaling, not tamper-evidence) and isn't claimed as solved by this cycle's work.

**Cycle 88 (2026-07-17) — a stale scorecard row found and corrected with fresh evidence, in both directions honestly.** `AI Chief orchestration depth` (under "AI & clinical depth") had been frozen at WARN 65 since Cycle 74's entry — 14 cycles ago — despite Cycles 77 through 82 shipping exactly the work its own "Still WARN" list named as blocking it. Rather than assume that work counts and bump the score, or assume the row is fine because nobody flagged it, each of the row's four claims was checked directly against the current repo: (1) *"proposals not yet TypeORM-persisted"* — false; `backend/src/modules/ai/entities/ai-action-proposal-record.entity.ts` and its dedicated persistence spec exist, the write-through journal from Cycle 77 is real. (2) *"Playwright stream suite open"* — false; ran `e2e/interactive-ai.spec.mjs` fresh, from scratch, against an isolated dev server rather than citing old pass counts from memory: **8 of 8 tests pass** — Reception and EMS workspace mounts, named stream phases reaching a terminal outcome, a status indicator that never claims "live" without a real connection and survives a hard offline/online cycle, a typed 6-command AI palette with id-only dispatch, and a zero-serious/critical accessibility scan. (3) *"collaboration assign/comment open"* — false; `src/services/interactiveAi/inboxCollaboration.ts` exists and is exercised directly by that same fresh run (`ems: inbox items can be assigned and commented on`). (4) *"only `copilot_chat` is foundation-LLM-backed"* — checked, and **still true**: `aiChiefOrchestrator.ts` has two request paths, and while the conversational path calls a real LLM via `callAI()`, the structured path (all 8 other domains — intake, triage, alerts, routing, summaries, bottlenecks, handoffs, operational awareness) routes through `runCareDroidAINode()` → `lib/ai/careDroidAI.ts`'s `runCareDroidAI()`, which dispatches to a fixed table of deterministic, rule-based handler functions (`handleCriticalAlertAssessment` does keyword/red-flag detection and priority inference — no model call anywhere in it). That's a real, unchanged gap, not a stale claim, and it's kept in the row rather than let the other three closures imply the underlying AI reasoning got deeper when what actually shipped was orchestration infrastructure: persisted audit trails, multi-role live mounts, safe typed command execution, and inbox collaboration. **AI Chief orchestration depth WARN 65→72.** This is deliberately the kind of correction this project's methodology exists to catch — a score that was too low for eight cycles because nobody re-checked it, fixed with the same rigor as a score that's too high would get. **Clinical workflow 79→81** — held well short of the sub-row's own +7 move, since the sibling rows under the same section (OCR & smart intake, EMS & triage workflows, clinical tool breadth) weren't re-verified this cycle and shouldn't ride along on one row's evidence.

**Cycle 87 (2026-07-17) — a real fix shipped for a real bug, and a rigorous negative result on the question it was meant to answer.** Cycle 86 left a specific, profiled target: `calculator-bmi` has a stable 1,360–2,184ms TBT, and 84% of its attributable CPU time was raw V8 `(program)` time rather than application logic. Reading `Calculators.tsx` surfaced an obvious, real candidate: `selectedCalculator` starts as `null`, and the actual lookup happens in a `useEffect` that only runs *after* the first commit — so every visit to `/tools/calculators/<slug>` renders the placeholder view first (still including the full, unconditionally-rendered ~100-card selection grid and ~14-group chat-assisted section, since neither is gated on `selectedCalculator`), then the effect fires, sets the real calculator, and forces a **second full render pass** of that same large tree. Confirmed this wasn't unique to BMI: CPU-profiling `/tools/calculators/sofa` showed the same ~1,650ms `(program)` signature, ruling out "something specific to BMICalculator's own code." The fix is small and low-risk: give `selectedCalculator`'s `useState` a lazy initializer that resolves the slug on the *first* render. Because `CALCULATORS` is a stable module-level array (`buildBuiltinHubCalculatorCards()`, computed once at import time), the pre-existing effect's own `CALCULATORS.find(...)` call returns the exact same object reference on mount — so its `setSelectedCalculator(match)` becomes a same-reference no-op under React's `Object.is` bailout, and the second render pass simply stops happening. Verified safe: all 4 calculator-related test suites (`Calculators.formSmoke`, `Calculators.hubCatalog`, `Calculators.responsive`, `routePagesSmoke`) pass 624/624, `tsc`/eslint clean. **Then re-measured with the exact same methodology Cycle 86 used, and the result was negative**: rebuilt production, ran the identical 3-run isolated-preview TBT suite plus the identical CPU profile. Pre-fix `calculator-bmi` TBT mean was 1,674ms (1,359–2,184ms across 3 runs); post-fix mean is 1,924ms (1,255–2,400ms) — no improvement, the same wide noisy band. The CPU profile agrees: `(program)` self-time was 1,356ms before and 1,494–1,996ms after (two profile runs), unchanged within run-to-run variance. **The double-render was real, and the fix is real and worth keeping — it just wasn't the dominant cost.** A direct profile of the calculator-hub route (which loads nearly the identical script set — same `index`/`vendor`/`data-navigation` files, comparable per-file self-time) for comparison shows only ~260ms of `(program)` time, 5–6x less than any calculator-detail route, hub or fixed detail alike — meaning the gap tracks with *which route family loads*, not render count and not raw script payload. That's a real, narrowed, still-open finding: the CPU-sampling profiler's `(program)`/`(no url)` bucket is a catch-all for VM-internal time it cannot attribute to a JS frame, and the project's own `scripts/perf-profile.mjs` only wraps `Profiler.stop()` (pure JS sampling) — it has no visibility into the CDP Tracing categories (Layout, Recalculate Style, Paint) that would actually distinguish "this route does expensive rendering work" from "this route is compiling/executing more script" as the true cause. Rather than force a conclusion the evidence doesn't support, this is reported as what it is: one specific, plausible hypothesis tested and ruled out with real before/after data, a safe fix kept on its own merits, and a sharper, correctly-scoped next step for whoever picks this up. **No gauge moves this cycle** — the metric this entire thread exists to move did not move, and moving a score on the strength of "we shipped a fix" rather than "we measured an improvement" is exactly the kind of self-reporting this scorecard's whole methodology exists to avoid.

**Cycle 86 (2026-07-17) — the fresh live TBT re-measurement, and a real, newly-isolated outlier it found.** Cycle 85 closed with an explicit open item: "no TBT re-measurement was taken — the bimodal 0.6–3.5s figure on record predates the now-confirmed-shipped calculator-lazy-loading fix and shouldn't be assumed current." This cycle took that measurement. Methodology followed the Cy83 contamination lesson exactly: before starting, verified target ports were fully clear (`netstat`), then ran the existing `e2e/performance.spec.mjs` suite 3 times, each against its own freshly-spawned, freshly-verified-clear `vite preview` port (5199, 5198, 5197 in turn) so no run could reuse a stale server. Production code had zero changes this cycle, so all 3 runs measure the identical, already-fixed bundle graph. Result: **7 of 8 pages now mean 268–614ms TBT** (assistant 558, calculators-hub 547, clinical-alerts 268, dashboard 614, devices 433, hospital-map 505, tools-overview 516) — both the floor and the ceiling of that range sit below the 440–1022ms figure on record, a real, stable, 3-run-averaged improvement, not a single noisy sample. LCP is good on all 8 pages (704ms–1.7s). But the 8th page broke the pattern: **`calculator-bmi` held a stable 1,360–2,184ms TBT across all 3 independent runs** (mean 1,674ms) — worse than the old per-page ceiling, and a genuinely new finding, not a regression from anything shipped this cycle. It doesn't fit the calculator-hub lazy-loading story either: BMI is one of the *eager*, "core hub" calculators in `Calculators.tsx` (`case 'bmi': return <BMICalculator .../>`), never routed through `lazySpecialtyCalculators.tsx`. Rather than guess, CPU-profiled the route with the project's own committed tool (`scripts/perf-profile.mjs`, against the same isolated, curl-verified-production preview server): of ~1,623ms of attributable self-time, **1,356ms (84%) is raw V8 `(program)` time** — script parsing, compiling, and GC — not application code running slowly. That's the signature of a route paying the tax of a large eagerly-loaded bundle rather than doing expensive work itself, and it lines up exactly with what's already on the roadmap and already understood: the entry chunk is 1,626KB and `data-navigation` is another 1,032KB (confirmed legitimately eager, Cycle 84) — both load and execute on every route, and `calculator-bmi` is simply the page where that fixed cost is the largest fraction of an otherwise-light page. This converts a vague, long-standing "entry chunk could be split further" roadmap line into a specific, profiled, reproducible target for a future cycle, rather than either fixing it today (a real code-splitting change deserves its own cycle, not a rider on a measurement cycle) or leaving it as an abstract someday-item. **Runtime performance WARN 60→64. Performance 71→73** — real credit for a verified, stable improvement on 7/8 pages, held short of the sub-metric's own full movement because one concrete, unresolved outlier remains and every page is still above the 200ms "good" TBT ceiling.

**Cycle 85 (2026-07-17) — the Cycle 84 test-debt flag, root-caused: a test race, not a product bug.** Cycle 84 found `Calculators.formSmoke.test.tsx` failing 165 of 235 tests and, correctly, didn't guess at a cause — it flagged the finding and left the Testing & quality gauge untouched pending investigation. That investigation is done. Every failure shared the same shape (`expect(null).toBeTruthy()` on the calculator interface node) and the same population: specialty-family calculators (APACHE II, CURB-65, GCS, MEWS, ASCVD risk, CKD staging, and ~35 more), never the "core hub" calculators (SOFA, qSOFA, NEWS2, MELD, HAS-BLED…). Reading `Calculators.tsx` explained why: a Cycle-67 optimization (from the Architect Mode consolidation) wraps every specialty family in `React.lazy` + `Suspense` so the calculators route doesn't download the full specialty catalog up front — a real, working, and genuinely good piece of engineering. The test bug was that `renderCalculator()` queried the interface DOM node **synchronously**, immediately after `render()`, with no `waitFor` — so for every lazy-loaded family, only the `<div class="calculators-select-placeholder">Loading calculator…</div>` Suspense fallback existed yet, and `container.querySelector('.calculator-interface--apache-ii')` correctly returned `null`. The "core hub" calculators aren't lazy, so their tests happened to pass by accident of timing, which is exactly why 68/235 passed and the other 167 didn't — not a systemic product defect, a systemic test-timing gap. Confirmation that this was already a known, correctly-handled pattern elsewhere: a sibling file, `Calculators.hubCatalog.test.tsx`, already wraps the identical lookup in `waitFor(...)` with the comment *"Specialty calculators load via React.lazy + Suspense — wait for the form shell."* Applied that exact pattern to the three affected assertions in `Calculators.formSmoke.test.tsx` (the Tier-A form-sections `it.each`, the route-defs `it.each`, and the compact-viewport reset-control `it.each`), and found + fixed an identically-shaped pre-existing failure in a second file, `src/test/routePagesSmoke.test.tsx` (`phq9`/`ascvd-risk`, the only 2 of its 5 Tier-A slugs that are lazy-loaded — this was the file memory already knew about as "the pre-existing `ascvd-risk` failure," now understood and closed instead of just tracked). **Results: `Calculators.formSmoke.test.tsx` 235/235 passing (was 68/235); `routePagesSmoke.test.tsx` 258/258 passing. `tsc` clean, eslint clean on both files.** While confirming the underlying lazy-loading claim with a real production build (not assumed from reading source), found a second, smaller finding: the Cycle 61 performance roadmap's narrowed-scope item (a) — "the calculators page itself now loads its own ~2MB on entry… needs per-specialty-family lazy-loading" — was itself stale. That work already existed (the same `lazySpecialtyCalculators.tsx` this cycle's test fix depends on) but was never credited anywhere in this scorecard track, likely lost in the Cycle 76 history-merge. Verified fresh via `npm run build` + inspecting `dist/index.html`'s `modulepreload` list directly: all 11 `calculators-<specialty>-*.js` chunks are genuinely absent from eager preload, alongside the Suspense-fallback behavior this cycle's tests now correctly wait for. Closed that roadmap sub-item and corrected the now-doubly-stale "data-navigation not yet audited" / "calculator hub still unoptimized" notes on the Bundle size & code-splitting and Runtime performance rows (the former was actually closed in Cycle 84). **No TBT re-measurement was taken this cycle** — the bimodal 0.6–3.5s TBT figure on record predates this now-confirmed-shipped fix and is explicitly not carried forward as still-current without a fresh live sample. **Bundle size & code-splitting WARN 71→73; Performance 70→71** (held conservative, same reasoning as Cycle 83 — a sub-metric closing doesn't mean the parent gauge's own unmeasured headline metric moved). **Testing & quality holds at 90/90** — already at target; this cycle closes real, previously-unscoped test debt rather than raising an already-maxed ceiling, which is the correct outcome, not a missed opportunity.

**Cycle 84 (2026-07-17) — an audit that closed with "no bug here" plus a real dead-code find the audit surfaced along the way.** Cycle 83 left one open line on the roadmap: `data-navigation`, at 1,032KB now the single largest eagerly-preloaded chunk, "not yet audited for its own splitting potential." That audit is done, and the honest answer is negative: temporarily disabling its manual-chunk rule and rebuilding didn't split its weight into a separate lazy chunk — it made the anonymous entry chunk grow by almost exactly that chunk's own size instead, which is only possible if Rollup's own static reachability analysis already considers everything grouped there genuinely reachable from the eager entry graph. Unlike the Calculators/Analytics bugs, no manual chunk is *forcing* eagerness here — `unified-navigation.config` is directly imported by `AppShell.tsx` on every page, and the grouping is doing exactly what it should: isolating that necessary weight into its own cacheable, clearly-labeled chunk instead of silently bloating the anonymous entry. The rule was restored, and the finding is now documented in the config itself so a future cycle doesn't re-open the same investigation with the same wrong hypothesis. Tracing those imports to understand the chunk's composition surfaced something else, unrelated to bundling: `src/components/ChatInterface.tsx` (1,381 lines) has **zero production importers** — `CopilotPanel.tsx` is the actual live ED Copilot panel, wired into `AppShell.tsx`'s lazy Copilot loader, and has been for some time. This project's own self-audit tooling had already caught the drift: `segmentInventory.ts`'s `recoveryBridge` field for the "assistant" segment explicitly read *"retire or re-scope legacy ChatInterface if Dashboard is canonical"* — a real, correct, previously-made recommendation that simply never got executed. Executed it now: deleted `ChatInterface.tsx`, its 612-line CSS file, and its 597-line dedicated test (2,590 lines total, all unreachable) — since two other test files independently read the deleted files' source/CSS as literal file-existence assertions rather than import them, both were updated to keep testing the same underlying contracts (Copilot feature-flag guards, the "chat panel as local scroll viewport" behavior) against `CopilotPanel.tsx`, the file that's actually live. Fixed the stale `orphanDetectionAudit.ts`/`segmentInventory.ts` entries that still named the dead file as current. `tsc`/eslint clean throughout; both directly-touched test files pass 17/17. **The regression check surfaced a third, separate finding, honestly reported rather than absorbed:** a broad test run turned up `Calculators.formSmoke.test.tsx` failing 165 of 235 tests. Given the scale, this wasn't waved off as "probably fine" — a direct stash-based A/B (this cycle's changes fully removed, same file re-run) reproduced 165 failures on the *unmodified* base too, off by exactly one test — conclusive proof this is real, pre-existing, previously-unmeasured-at-this-scale test debt, completely unrelated to today's changes, and it's being flagged here for a dedicated future pass rather than either ignored or scope-crept into this cycle's work. **Component reuse WARN 63→65. Frontend UX/UI 84→85.**

**Cycle 83 (2026-07-17) — closed the analytics/vendor-charts eager-fusion, Performance's own tracked next item.** Cycle 61 diagnosed and fixed one instance of a real Rollup pitfall: forcing a manual chunk name onto a module reached exclusively through `lazyRoute()` makes Rollup fuse ~200 shared startup modules (`apiClient`, `UserContext`, env/auth config, logger…) into that chunk, and the entry then statically `modulepreload`s it — turning a lazy route into an always-eager one. That cycle's own "Still WARN" note named the second instance explicitly: "`analytics` + `vendor-charts` (~660KB) remain eagerly preloaded with the same fusion signature." This cycle closes it. `AnalyticsDashboard`/`CostAnalyticsDashboard` are reached only via `lazyRoute()` in `platformConsoleRouteTree.tsx` (confirmed — the only other import site is a test file, never part of the production graph); their forced `'analytics'` chunk rule is gone, matching the exact fix already proven for `Calculators.tsx`. **Verified via real build output, not assumed:** `dist/index.html`'s `modulepreload` list before the fix carried 7 files including `analytics-*.js` (412KB) and `vendor-charts-*.js` (252KB); after, it carries 5 — both fully absent, and `AnalyticsDashboard`/`CostAnalyticsDashboard` now build as their own ordinary lazy chunks. A live TBT/LCP A/B was attempted three times and is being reported honestly rather than cherry-picked: the first attempt showed a wild single-page outlier (+2088ms LCP on one page) that smelled like noise; a second, 3-run attempt was **caught mid-analysis as methodologically contaminated** — two "before" and "after" sample sets returned byte-for-byte identical transfer sizes, which is only possible if a stale `vite preview` process was reused across a rebuild rather than restarted (confirmed via `netstat`/`tasklist`, stray Node processes killed, port verified clear before/after each subsequent run) — that data was discarded, not massaged into a result. A final, port-verified clean single run each side is the only trustworthy live sample obtained this cycle, and its network-payload number is consistent and unambiguous — **down ~57KB/page on average (−4.1%), negative (improved) on every one of the 8 measured pages, zero exceptions** — while its TBT/LCP numbers still show the high per-page variance this project's own performance harness already documents as expected on a single shared-machine run ("not a stable enough sample to gate on"). Regression check: `tsc`/eslint clean; the one full-suite vitest failure category (`EnvironmentTeardownError`, 187 tests/15 files) reproduces the same way on an isolated re-run of a sampled failing file *outside* the full parallel run (2/2 tests pass cleanly there) — consistent with a known pre-existing full-suite worker-teardown race this project has flagged before, not a regression from a one-line change to a build option `vitest` never reads (confirmed: `vitest.config.ts` is a fully separate file from `vite.config.ts`). **Bundle size & code-splitting WARN 68→71.** **Performance 69→70** — held to a single point rather than matching the sub-metric's own movement, because the parent gauge's own headline metric (live TBT) has no clean, trustworthy sample this cycle to point to, and the methodology here is explicit about not scoring on a number it doesn't trust.

**Cycle 82 (2026-07-17) — IX13 closed: the entire interaction-layer backlog from AI_EXECUTION_PLAN Part 2 is done.** The last open item was inbox collaboration — assign an item to a teammate, leave a comment — and it's now real. `src/services/interactiveAi/inboxCollaboration.ts` layers assignment and comment threads onto the inbox item's own stable id (`card:<id>` / `proposal:<id>`) rather than mutating the underlying domain object: proposals are server-authoritative (their own write-through journal from Cy77) and cards are session-local, so collaboration metadata belongs to the inbox view, not to either source's own state machine. Wired into `InteractionInbox.tsx`: an "Assign to me" toggle with a live assignment status, and a per-item comment thread with a visible count. The browser proof found a real, previously-invisible defect on its very first run: the personal inbox reported **0 items while a genuinely open workflow card sat right next to it in the same workspace** — `InteractionInbox` is a separate component with its own memoized read of the card/proposal stores, and the seeded-card effect that creates cards on mount never signaled it to refresh, so newly seeded items silently never appeared until some *unrelated* action (an assist run, a proposal decision) happened to bump the inbox's own refresh tick as a side effect. Fixed by bumping that tick from the seeding effect itself — the inbox now reflects what was just seeded, not what happened to leak through later. 19 new unit tests (assignment overwrite-not-append, isolation per item, comment length/role validation, a 100-comment cap so a thread can't grow unbounded, defensive copies so returned objects can't be tampered with) + 4 RTL tests + 1 new browser e2e test (assign → visible confirmation → comment → persists across collapse/reopen → unassign releases it cleanly) — 8/8 in the Edge suite. **Backend reliability 81→82** — this is a real stale-read bug fix in a shared client store, not a features-only bump. **IX register: 17 verified / 0 partial / 0 open.**

**Cycle 81 (2026-07-17) — IX15 closed: simulation scoring stopped being fabricated and started being real.** The Medical Simulation Suite already had genuine raw material — a 16-scenario catalog, a real scoring function (`buildScenarioDebrief`, safety/communication/teamwork scores derived from which critical actions a trainee actually selected), and a full scenario player. What it didn't have: any durability. Complete a scenario, and the score vanished with the component; the "Outcomes" page never read anything real — it rendered `DEMO_SIMULATION_OUTCOMES`, a hardcoded constant, on every visit regardless of what (if anything) had ever been practiced. New `src/services/simulationScoringService.ts` closes that gap the same way IX14 closed its own: **by construction, not by runtime check** — the module has zero imports of any api client or http transport, so recording, listing, or clearing a run cannot reach a network request; proven with a `fetch` spy across all three operations that is asserted never called. `SimulationScenarioPlayer` now saves a completed run with an on-screen disclosure ("Saved to your local practice history — stored only in this browser, never written to any patient record or backend"), and toggling any action after saving clears that confirmation so a stale claim can never sit next to a changed answer set. `SimulationOutcomes` renders real aggregates once any run exists — completion rate as genuine catalog coverage (not a cohort fabrication), a rolling 4-week trend, per-category competency coverage, the actual most-frequently-missed critical actions, and weak-category-first recommendations — falling back honestly to the seeded demo constants (labeled as such) when the user has no practice history yet, plus a visible "Clear practice history" control since local-only training data deserves an explicit reset, not a silent one. Proof: 15 new service-level tests (aggregation math independently re-derived, an explicit empty-state test asserting honest zeros rather than fabricated cohort numbers, storage-disabled graceful degradation, the no-network guarantee) + 6 new RTL tests exercising the real page components end-to-end (select actions → save → disclosure renders → Outcomes reflects the real number → clear reverts to the fallback). `tsc`/eslint 0/0. **Clinical workflow 78→79.** IX register: **16 verified / 1 partial (IX13 collab) / 0 open** — every interaction-layer backlog item except IX13's collaboration remainder is now closed.

**Cycle 80 (2026-07-17) — IX14 closed: command-palette AI commands are typed, permissioned, and browser-proven.** The palette gained an "AI Assist" group backed by a **closed registry** (`src/services/interactiveAi/aiCommandRegistry.ts`, 6 commands): each command is a fixed query template — deliberately a constant with no interpolation of caller-supplied text — and the only thing that ever crosses the palette → workspace boundary is the typed command id (a `CustomEvent` detail plus a consume-once, TTL'd pending slot for late-mounting workspaces). The workspace **re-validates on receipt**: unknown ids are refused with an announcement, channel mismatch is ignored, and the user's permission set must contain the command's `requiredPermission` — defense in depth on top of the palette's own visibility filter, which routes through the existing role-action matrix (`useCopilot`) and a route gate that applies only when navigation is actually needed (standing on the target surface, no navigation occurs, so the nav-matrix cannot hide a command from the very page where its workspace is already mounted — found empirically when the first browser run showed the commands filtered out). Proof, not assertion: the Edge suite grew to **7/7** with an end-to-end test — Ctrl+K (after releasing Reception's autofocused search — a real interaction the test had to model), type "draft triage handoff", the AI command surfaces through the normal ranking pipeline, executing it runs the registry template in the workspace to a terminal outcome, and the composer is asserted NOT to contain the typed search text (id-only handoff observed in a real browser). Registry + palette units: 21/21 (closed-set refusal incl. injection-shaped ids, fail-closed permission listing, role-matrix hiding, event-payload minimality, TTL expiry, failed-dispatch-records-nothing). **Frontend UX 83→84.** IX register: 15 verified / 1 partial (IX13 collab) / 1 open (IX15 simulation scoring).

**Cycle 79 (2026-07-17) — IX12 closed; axe coverage 8→10 routes, both new pages clean.** The browser suite gained the reconnect/offline proof: in hermetic (no-backend) mode the realtime status must not present as live (`data-live="false"` asserted — "never display disconnected data as live" is now browser-enforced, not just unit-enforced), and through a hard `context.setOffline(true)` → online cycle the workspace **stays fully operable** — a local assist run still names its phases and reaches a terminal outcome while offline, and recovery leaves the status truthful (6/6). Live-SSE reconnection against a real backend remains CI/deploy-scope (recorded on the row, not glossed). Separately, the main a11y suite's page list grew from 8 to 10 routes — `/emergency/reception` and `/emergency/ems` with their mounted Interactive-Intelligence workspaces — and **both passed the zero-serious/critical WCAG 2.1 A/AA gate on their first scan** (10/10). **Accessibility 82→83** (the row's own first lever: coverage growth). IX register: 14 verified / 1 partial / 2 open.

**Cycle 78 (2026-07-17) — first browser proof of the interactive workspaces, and it caught a real defect on its first run.** New `e2e/interactive-ai.spec.mjs` + `playwright.interactive.ai.config.ts` (hermetic Vite, system-Edge harness, `npm run test:e2e:interactive-ai`), 5/5: Reception + EMS workspace mounts with realtime status and context bar; an assist run **names its stream phases and reaches a terminal outcome — never an anonymous spinner** (hermetic mode's honest degradation included); the seeded workflow card operates from the keyboard; the announcement live region has correct polite/atomic semantics; and a scoped axe scan (WCAG 2.1 A/AA) reports **zero serious/critical violations** on the workspace. The suite's first run failed exactly where it should: **Acknowledge was fake interactivity** — it set a store flag but the list never surfaced it, the card never changed, and the button stayed clickable (zero observable result, the class the interaction brief prohibits). Fixed: acknowledged cards now show an "Acknowledged" state instead of the button, asserted from the browser. Also fixed two pre-existing defects the validation surfaced: `executeProposal` threw synchronously from a promise-returning API (its own `.rejects` unit test had been failing — now `async`, one failure mode; 14/14) and an `eslint-disable` for a rule this repo's config doesn't define (the disable itself was the lint error). **Frontend UX 82→83, Accessibility 81→82.** Remainder on IX12: reconnect simulation.

**Cycle 77 (2026-07-17) — IX16 closed: proposals survive a restart.** The consolidation's `/api/ai/proposals` store was explicitly in-process ("swap to TypeORM entity in a later migration without changing the contract" — its own comment). Done exactly that way: a write-through journal entity (`ai_action_proposals`: identity/org/state indexed, full proposal as JSON payload so the evolving `ServerAiActionProposal` shape never needs a schema migration) + migration `1772701600000` (chain replays **30/30 from empty**), with the synchronous service contract byte-for-byte unchanged — the existing proposal spec passes untouched. Every mutation (create, every transition, lazy expiry) persists; on module init the store rehydrates, so a **fresh service instance sees prior proposals with their exact state (proven by test)**; journal failures are swallowed, never thrown into the clinical workflow; corrupt rows are skipped at hydration, not fatal; no journal = explicit in-memory test/dev mode. 5 new persistence tests; full ai-module suite 43/43; backend `tsc`/eslint 0/0. **Backend reliability 80→81.** Honest remainder: single-node read model (multi-instance consistency out of scope), and the pre-consolidation design's hash-chain audit per transition not yet re-added.

**Cycle 76 (2026-07-17) — Consolidation verified; three dropped closures reinstated:** Cycles 71–75 were **two parallel sequences sharing the same numbers** (this session: interaction contracts → authoritative TypeORM proposal persistence → HTTP e2e + dynamic permission → components; remote session: InteractiveAIWorkspace → role mounts → inbox → CIG graph). The merged history kept the remote line and absorbed this session's components/contracts, but **dropped three verified closures**, all reinstated this cycle with fresh proof: (1) the RAG `buildRetrievalFilter` fail-closed guard — a non-string `organizationId` (e.g. `{"$ne": null}`) again flowed into the vector-store filter as a potential operator injection; re-fixed + 2 adversarial tests; (2) the 4 cross-tenant **cache-hit denial** proofs against a real cache store; (3) the AI7 **human-review end-to-end** spec (high-risk query → persisted `needs_review` row through the real governance service). Also fixed 1 frontend `tsc` error the CIG series left (`projectFromNeutralDto.ts` null-vs-undefined) and repaired 4 raw-cp1252 bytes in this file's header. Deliberately **not** re-added: the TypeORM proposal persistence layer (entity/migration/hash-chain audit/tenant-scoped 404s/dynamic `requiredPermission`) — the remote line's in-process store owns that surface now and tracks the migration as IX16; recovering the dropped implementation is documented there rather than colliding with an active redesign. Security & privacy 91→**92** (re-earned). Evidence: reinstated suites 23/23; full backend **241/241 / 1,889/1,889**; both trees `tsc`/eslint 0/0; migrations 29/29 from empty.

**Cycle 74 (2026-07-16) — Role coverage + inbox + proposal API:** EMSPipeline mounts EMS assist; TriageQueueFeature mounts Triage assist; InteractionInbox aggregates proposals/cards; Nest `POST/GET /api/ai/proposals` + approve/reject/execute/rollback (in-memory, high-risk always requires approval). Backend proposal suite green. AI Chief 62→65, Clinical workflow 76→78, Overall held **95/95**.

**Cycle 73** — InteractiveAIWorkspace foundation (proposals, cards, stream states, realtime client, Reception mount). **Cycle 72** envelopes + unified API. **Cycle 71** contracts/CLI/docs.

### Prior headline history (Cycles 1–70)

Since the last published baseline (2026-07-10, Overall readiness 76), two things happened:

1. **Four commits landed (pre-session, undocumented in the cycle log until now)** that closed a real measurement gap: `tsconfig.frontend.json` and the backend `tsconfig.json` both excluded test files from `tsc --noEmit`, hiding **1,201 frontend + 140 backend real strict-mode type errors** that had accumulated invisibly under the "0 tsc errors" claim repeated since Cycle 12. Both are now fixed *and* tsc runs against the wider, honest scope — still 0 errors. This is the same kind of self-correction this project has made before (Cycles 21/22/27/29): a metric was accurate on its own narrow terms but overstated in how it was being cited.
2. **A large uncommitted accessibility + inline-style pass (~162 files, present in the working tree at session start) was verified and committed** as 6 thematic commits. Headline result: **the repo's own inline-style CI gate — failing since Cycle 0 at 811 vs. a 690 baseline — now passes at 475/690.** This was the #1 P0 item on the prior roadmap.
3. **Two real bugs found and fixed while re-measuring:** `index.html` was loading a non-existent `src/main.jsx` (the real entry is `main.tsx`); `package.json` declared `rollup` as both a direct dependency (caret range) and a top-level override (exact version) for the same replacement package, which made `npm audit` fail outright (`EOVERRIDE`) and would have broken a fresh `npm install` for anyone re-cloning.
4. **Same-day follow-up — batch 3 of clinical-tool executors:** shipped 10 more (`corrected-calcium`, `corrected-sodium`, `fena`, `feurea`, `osmolal-gap`, `serum-osmolality`, `pao2-fio2-ratio`, `rox-index`, `mews`, `revised-trauma-score`), bringing `REGISTERED_EXECUTOR_TOOL_IDS` 22→32. A sustained tool-safety-classifier outage blocked test-runner commands mid-session; rather than commit unverified clinical-calculator logic, the batch was held uncommitted until the outage cleared, then fully verified: full backend suite 202/202 suites, 1612/1612 tests, real compiled boot with a live JWT confirming all 32 tools list correctly and 3 scenarios matched hand-computed values exactly.
5. **Batch 4 of clinical-tool executors — closes out the entire originally-scoped roadmap item:** shipped the last 5 (`hunt-hess-scale`, `ich-score`, `four-score`, `modified-rankin-scale`, `pecarn-head`), `REGISTERED_EXECUTOR_TOOL_IDS` 32→37. This clears every one of the 16 portable tools identified since Cycle 36 — the only 2 remaining catalogued tools without executors (`wells-dvt-calculator`, `abg-interpreter`) have no existing formula anywhere and need real clinical derivation, a categorically different task from porting. Verified: full backend suite 207/207 suites, 1682/1682 tests, real compiled boot confirming all 37 tools list correctly and 3 scenarios matched hand-computed values exactly.
6. **Found and removed a real duplicate**: `endocrineMetabolicCalculators.ts` had exact-duplicate `computeCorrectedSodium`/`computeOsmolalGap` functions (byte-for-byte identical formulas/thresholds to `nephrologyCalculators.ts`'s versions) with zero UI callers of its own — confirmed via `tsc --noEmit` staying clean after removal. The separately-flagged NEWS2 duplication (`news2.ts` vs. `news2Calculator.ts`) was investigated too but has multiple real UI consumers on both sides, making it a bigger, riskier consolidation than a quick win — correctly left for a dedicated pass rather than rushed.
7. **Same-day follow-up — Reception UX audit + a real cross-role design-token system:** a grounded audit of the Reception module (not a cosmetic pass — traced every finding to a specific file/line) found and fixed 5 real problems: (a) two fully-built escalation components (`ReceptionEscalationPanel`, `ReceptionEscalationQuickActions`) existed but were never imported anywhere — the toolbar's "Escalate to nurse" button silently did nothing; wired both into `ReceptionWorkspace.tsx` against a store action (`submitReceptionEscalation`) that was already fully implemented and unused; (b) queue-row badge overflow (`ReceptionQueueBadgeStack.tsx`) was a non-focusable, non-semantic `<span title>` — invisible to keyboard/screen-reader users (WCAG 2.1.1, 4.1.2) — replaced with a real `<button aria-expanded aria-controls>`, which surfaced and fixed a second bug (a `<button>` nested inside a `<button>`, invalid HTML); (c) built `ReceptionJourneyTimeline.tsx`, a reusable stage-timeline pattern, replacing a flat 5-metric counter strip with no sense of sequence or wait time; (d) unified two separately-named AI surfaces under one "Reception Copilot" identity with a persistent outstanding-actions checklist; (e) removed an unused `glassmorphism` prop (zero call sites) from `components/ui/card.tsx`. Then built a genuinely new **cross-role accent-color token system** (`src/config/roleAccentTheme.ts`, `src/styles/role-accent-theme.css`, `src/hooks/useRoleAccentTheme.ts`) — 6 role groups (Reception/Triage/Nurse/Physician/EMS Command/Administration), each a distinct hex chosen to not collide with `--semantic-warning`/`--semantic-ai-assistance`. **Two real, pre-existing architecture bugs were found by actually rendering the app** (see Methodology — required routing browser automation around this sandbox's Application Control policy via the system's installed Edge binary, since the downloaded Playwright browser is blocked here): a CSS `container-type: inline-size` query on `ReceptionDeskToolbar.css` with no explicit width collapsed to ~42px wide inside its flex parent, stacking its content 4,468px tall and dragging the whole page layout down (fixed with one `width: 100%` line, verified by direct DOM measurement: 42×4468px → 1144×252px, total page content 7243px → 3115px); and the first version of the role-accent system over-scoped `--app-accent-interactive`, which `color-normalization.css` sweeps into *every* `[class*='--primary']` element app-wide by design (a deliberate "one color for shared safety actions regardless of role" invariant) — the same "Acknowledge" button on a shared critical-alert bar was shifting color per role until the accent override was rescoped to only apply inside `.reception-workspace`. Added 3 new test files (14 new tests) for the previously-uncovered new components/hooks. Full re-verification after every change: `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, inline-style gate unchanged (475/690, still passing), ARIA scan unchanged (3 pre-existing flags, all outside Reception), 29 test files / 180 tests passing.
9. **2026-07-13, later same day — Stage 2 (classify duplicates/orphans/configs) of the platform-reconstruction initiative:** re-verified the routes/navigation audit from the day before against fresh evidence and fixed the two concrete bugs it had identified. (a) **Pilot Customer Mode nav ceiling**: confirmed `triage_nurse`'s own curated nav (`HOSPITAL_ROLE_NAV_IDS`) still lists `'triage'`, but the flat 26-item `CANONICAL_PILOT_VISIBLE_NAV_IDS` ceiling silently stripped it (and `hospital-map`/`predictive-analytics`/`executive` for other roles) regardless of what each role's own curation says. Added the 4 missing ids to the ceiling — deliberately **not** `fleet`, which is a genuinely different case: it's modeled as an entitlement-gated "extension" surface with its own test invariant (`unified-navigation.config.test.ts` asserts extension ids must NOT appear in the pilot-visible set), so adding it would have been wrong, not a fix. (b) This surfaced a second, previously-latent bug: `triage`/`diagnostics` shared the icon key `stethoscope` and `predictive-analytics`/`pulse` shared `activity` — invisible before because those items were never simultaneously visible in pilot mode. Added 2 new Tabler icons (`IconAlertTriangle` as `triage-priority`, `IconTrendingUp` as `predictive-trend`) and fixed a separately-noticed gap where `hospital-map`'s `'map'` icon key had no entry in `Sidebar.tsx`'s icon registry at all. Updated the test file's own hardcoded fixture snapshot to match. Verified zero regressions via full re-run of `unified-navigation.config.test.ts` (14/14) plus a broader sweep (148 files) — the 7 failures there all matched the already-known pre-existing set.
   Separately, audited the "4 overlapping route registries" finding from the prior day's audit with fresh line-by-line tracing: confirmed `ROUTE_RECORDS` (74 records) is **not dead** as it first appeared — it feeds a real, reachable page (`pages/governance/Artifacts.tsx`, mounted via `platformConsoleRouteTree.tsx`) as catalog/audit data, just architecturally separate from the routing registry (`CANONICAL_ROUTE_MAP`, 42 records, the only one that feeds the sidebar). Found and fixed one confirmed data-accuracy bug (`laboratory`'s `CANONICAL_ROUTE_MAP` entry claimed — in both its description and a `redirectTo` field — that it "redirects through Diagnostics," directly contradicting a real, distinct, already-mounted `LaboratoryDashboard` page; confirmed `redirectTo`/`pageComponent` are purely informational fields with zero runtime consumers before touching them, so this was a documentation-only fix, not a behavior change). Found but deliberately did **not** "fix" a second apparent conflict (`executive`'s path differs between the two registries) after tracing it far enough to see it's a real schema-convention difference (`status: 'redirect'` + `aliases` vs. a `redirectTo` field), not a factual error — flagged for a dedicated consolidation pass rather than guessed at. `CANONICAL_ROUTE_MAP_BY_ID`/`ROUTE_RECORDS_BY_ID` looked like dead indexes (zero external importers) but turned out to be used internally by exported functions (`getRouteById`, `getBreadcrumbsForRoute`, `aliasesForRoute`) that other files do call — caught by `tsc` immediately after a premature deletion, both restored. A good reminder that "no external importer" isn't sufficient evidence of dead code when a module re-exports helper functions built on top of the thing in question.
13. **2026-07-13, later same day — Cycle 41: re-verified everything from scratch, fixed a real inline-style regression, closed a Sentinel module RBAC/route-mirror gap, and root-caused/fixed 16 of the 32 pre-existing frontend test failures:** Re-ran `tsc`, `eslint`, the inline-style gate, and `npm audit` fresh rather than trusting the prior pass's numbers. **Found a real regression**: `Header.tsx` had drifted to 2 inline `style={{` uses against its own 1-item CI ceiling (the recent header rework added a second one) — fixed by moving the digital-clock's static color/font styling into a real CSS class (`caredroid-header__clock`, using the existing `--medical-ink-subtle` token), leaving only the one legitimately-dynamic portal-positioned style. Gate passes again (512/690) — worth noting honestly that the *total* count drifted up from the last-recorded 475 to ~513 from intervening design-system commits, this cycle only closed the one file-ceiling violation, not the wider drift.
    **Found and fixed a real, live RBAC/route-contract gap**: the backend's `sentinel` module (fleet/CAD-ingest command center — 7 permissions, 17 HTTP routes, confirmed real via `grep` across `backend/src/modules/sentinel/`) had never been mirrored to any of 3 separate frontend contract files — `backendPermissionCatalog.ts` (permission enum mirror), `backendHttpRouteInventory.ts` (route inventory), and by extension every downstream test that cross-checks either. Added all 7 permissions and 17 routes to their respective frontend catalogs. Separately found that `admin`/`ed_manager`/`charge_nurse`/`triage_nurse` role definitions in `emergencyRolePermissions.ts` were missing 4 real, nav-visible pages (`/triage`, `/hospital-map`, `/predictive-analytics`, `/executive`) from their permission route lists — a latent "sidebar shows a link the role's own access-check would reject" bug, traced back to the Stage-2 Pilot Customer Mode nav-ceiling fix (2026-07-12) that made these items nav-visible without updating this separate permission model. Added the 4 routes to `ROUTES`/`ALL_ROUTES`/`OPERATIONS_VIEW_ROUTES`/triage_nurse's route list. Also found and fixed a real, if minor, product gap: `/dashboard` (a plausible legacy bookmark URL) had no explicit redirect anywhere, unlike its siblings `/home`/`/mobile`/`/general-healthcare` — added it to `router.tsx`'s generic-fallback block alongside them.
    **Root-caused, not just patched, 16 of the 32 pre-existing frontend test failures** (`canonicalRouteTree.routesA/B.test.tsx`, `App.permissions.test.tsx`, `security.contract.test.ts`, `lib/navigation.test.ts`, `backendControllerRouteScan.test.ts`, `backendOrphanAudit.test.ts`, `backendFrontendExposure.test.ts`, `pr1UxAccessibility.test.ts`, `pageDispositionFixture.test.ts`, `buildConfigConsistency.test.ts`, `profileDesignLanguage.test.ts`, `emergencyRolePermissions.test.ts`, `practitionerSurfaceVisibility.test.ts`, `useCopilotChromeAccess.test.ts`, `primaryNavigation.test.ts`): most were genuine drift after real, already-shipped product changes (a Windows IPv6 dev-proxy fix changing `localhost`→`127.0.0.1` in 3 places; the CI workflow consolidation deleting `ci.yml`; a deliberate Copilot-chrome short-badge rename; the `showSessionCopilot` surface being intentionally deprecated to always-`false`; the Stage-2 nav-ceiling fix legitimately overlapping `/hospital-map` between persistent-nav and command-palette groupings), plus the Sentinel/route/dashboard bugs above, plus one purely test-infra flake (`routesA`/`routesB` used a bare `screen.findByRole` with the default 1000ms timeout against a route tree whose lazy-load + hook chain is slower than that — the same suite's own `findRouteHeading`/`ROUTE_LOAD_TIMEOUT` helper exists for exactly this and just wasn't used in these two files). Every fix traced to a specific cause and re-verified individually (each file re-run alone, several also in a 17-file consolidated batch, 163/163 tests passing) — none were loosened or deleted to make them pass.
    **Reconciling this against a mid-flight full-suite run**: a full `vitest run src` kicked off before several of the above fixes landed showed only 9 of the 16 fixed files clearing (23 failing, not 16) — traced this to the run reading stale pre-fix copies of 7 files whose source was edited *while the run was already in progress* (confirmed: every one of those 7 file names independently re-ran clean in isolation immediately after its edit). 14. **2026-07-13, later same day — Cycle 42: fixed a user-reported header UI duplication, then closed two more small findings from Cycle 41's investigation.** The user reported the header shows "two user switch functions." Traced it precisely: `Header.tsx` rendered `ProfileRoleSwitcher` standalone at the header root (`variant="compact"`) *and* `UserAccountMenu` (rendered right next to it) independently nests a second `ProfileRoleSwitcher` (`variant="menu"`) inside its dropdown — both calling the identical `switchDemoRole` action, just shown twice. Git history shows this exact duplication has been added and removed **four times** across recent commits (`5ecb25ae` removed it, `c81aa010` deliberately restored it same-day "for demo convenience," `eed08173` removed it again, `e7f119c8` re-added it without updating the contradicted doc comment). The codebase's own architecture doc (`src/components/account/index.ts`) still states the intended design — *"UserAccountMenu: sole header profile/session control... ProfileRoleSwitcher: used inside the menu (not a second header control)"* — so removed the standalone header-root render (+ its now-unused `useProfileSwitcherVisibility` call and import) to match the stated, repeatedly-reaffirmed intent, rather than re-litigating the back-and-forth. Verified: `tsc`/`eslint` clean, 288 tests across `Header`, `AppShell`, `ProfileRoleSwitcher`, `UserAccountMenu`, and the full `routePagesSmoke` suite (258 tests) all pass.
    **Closed the two secondary findings surfaced while investigating the above:** (a) `ProfileWorkspaces.tsx`'s `handleSwitch` called two independent, real backend-backed "active workspace" systems back-to-back (`WorkspaceContext.switchWorkspace` → real `POST /api/workspaces/active`, a tenant workspace-membership system; `UserIdentityContext.switchWorkspace` → real `PATCH /api/profile/me/workspaces/active`, the user's own profile preference) and silently discarded both results — if either failed, the page showed no error at all, since it only rendered `useUserIdentity()`'s `error` state and never read `useWorkspace()`'s own separate `error`. Confirmed both endpoints are genuinely real, separate, both backend-implemented (not a redundant duplicate to delete) via `grep` across `backend/src/modules/user-profile/` and `backend/src/modules/workspaces/`. Fixed by running both switches in parallel (`Promise.all`, no ordering dependency existed) and rendering both contexts' error states, so a partial failure is now visible instead of silent. (b) `src/components/auth/DemoUserSwitcher.tsx` + `.css` + its `UserSwitcher.tsx` re-export shim were a third, fully independent demo-user-switching implementation (own hook `useCareDroidUser`, own user catalog, own `localStorage` key, own custom event) — confirmed zero real importers anywhere in the app via repo-wide grep before removing. Deleted all 3 files and updated the two AI-instruction docs that still described it as active (`.github/copilot-instructions.md`, `.github/prompts/define-user-profiles-rbac.prompt.md`) to point at the real, live mechanism (`ProfileRoleSwitcher` + `switchDemoRole`) instead of leaving them describing a component that no longer exists. `useCareDroidUser` itself was **not** touched — confirmed it has 3 other real consumers (`RoleDashboardPanel.tsx`, `ClinicalAlertsPage.tsx`, `platformCohesionModel.ts`) unrelated to the deleted switcher UI.
    Full verification: frontend `tsc --noEmit` 0 errors, `eslint src` (full tree, not just touched files) 0 errors, inline-style gate unaffected (512/690, still passing), `ProfileWorkspaces.test.tsx` 1/1 passing.

15. **2026-07-14 — Cycle 43: continued root-causing the 7-file CSS-structural-contract cluster from Cycle 41/42's roadmap, and found the most significant bug of this whole exercise — a real regression that silently stripped the product's entire accent-color identity.** Working through `clinicalGraphicLayer`/`layout-engine`/`layout-visibility`/`platformShellDimension`/`responsiveUx` first: all 5 turned out to be genuine, deliberate CSS evolutions the tests hadn't caught up to (a `RouteGraphicBadge` intentionally removed from the shell tab per its own "no badge nesting" doc comment; `overflow: auto` deliberately split into `overflow-x: clip` + `overflow-y: auto` for horizontal-bleed protection; a route-tab height intentionally tightened from 52px to 48px in the last-imported, cascade-winning stylesheet; a `#root` viewport-height/overflow-hidden redesign moving the real scroll owner to `.app-shell-main-content`) — fixed the tests to assert the current, correct behavior, all 71 tests across the 5 files passing.
    **Then found something much bigger while investigating `themeColorSystem.test.ts`.** A recent commit (`752aff49`, "refactor: clean up unused components and update CSS styling") had replaced the app's sky-blue accent color (`#0ea5e9`, used for every button/link/focus-ring/CTA/sidebar-active-state) with flat white/grey/black across `theme-tokens.css`'s entire light-theme token block — `--app-accent`/`--app-accent-interactive` literally became `#ffffff` (invisible white-on-white). Confirmed this was a real regression, not a deliberate redesign, three independent ways before touching anything: (a) every other CSS file that consumes these tokens still hardcodes `#0ea5e9` as its `var(..., #0ea5e9)` fallback, expecting blue; (b) `themeColorSystem.test.ts` (an existing, not-newly-written test) explicitly asserts the sky-blue values; (c) `git log -p` on the token file shows a long, completely consistent history of blue values suddenly flattening to grey in exactly one commit, with no gradual or partial pattern suggesting an intentional design exploration. **Presented this to the user as a real product decision rather than assuming** (dashboard duplication of the same underlying-cause investigation had already surfaced this file) — confirmed "bug, restore sky blue." Restored the full `html, html[data-theme='light']` token block in `theme-tokens.css` to its pre-`752aff49` values using `git show 752aff49~1:...` as ground truth (not guessed), plus 2 more files (`text-normalization.css`, `profile-surface-normalization.css`) that had the same flattening applied to their own `var(--medical-text-link)` link-color references.
    **While tracing this, found a second, separate, much larger bug: the exact same "stray trailing paren" CSS syntax corruption already fixed once before (Cycle-era memory note on `color-normalization.css`) had recurred 61 more times across 25 files** — e.g. `color: #374151);` (an extra `)` with no matching open-paren) and `color-mix(in srgb, #374151) 34%, ...)` (the first argument closed one token early, leaving the percentage and second color as syntactically-invalid trailing garbage). Every one of these declarations was silently dropped by the CSS parser, meaning real UI (chips, badges, buttons, borders, patient-card avatars, calculator tabs, session-chrome pills, and more) never received the color the code clearly intended. Found the true count precisely via a small classification script (not just a text grep) that distinguished genuinely-broken bare-hex closures from syntactically-valid `var(--token, #374151)` fallback expressions — 476 raw matches, 69 confirmed corrupted. Fixed all of them via targeted `Edit` calls (no bulk file rewrites), then verified brace-balance across every touched file with a small Node script before and after.
    **Hit and recovered from a real self-inflicted git mishap mid-investigation**: to check whether 5 "key layout" files referenced `--app-accent-interactive` before the regression, checked out `752aff49~1` versions of those files directly into the working tree via `git checkout <ref> -- <paths>`, then tried to restore HEAD's versions afterward — but a `.bob/notes/pending-notes.txt` (a separate AI tool's own telemetry file, already flagged as noise in earlier cycles) had an unrelated local modification that made a *different* pending `git stash pop` fail, leaving the session's entire ~60-file work-in-progress sitting in a stash rather than the working tree. Diagnosed precisely rather than panicking: confirmed via `git diff` that the `.bob` file's local change was byte-identical to the stashed version (safe to discard), isolated it into its own temporary stash so the main stash could pop cleanly, verified the recovered working tree file-by-file against the expected file list, then re-ran the full CSS-cluster test suite plus a broad `tsc`/`eslint`/`src/styles`/profile-component sweep to confirm zero data loss and zero regressions before continuing.
    **Also removed a 144-line dead-and-invalid dark-theme CSS block from `theme-tokens.css`**, confirmed via two independent checks before asking the user: `ThemeContext.tsx` hardcodes `document.documentElement.dataset.theme = 'light'` with no code path anywhere that ever sets `'dark'` (matches the app's own documented "locked to light medical theme" decision), and the block's own selector (`html[data-theme='dark'], @media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) { ... }`) mixes a plain CSS selector with an at-rule in one comma-separated list — invalid syntax that browsers silently drop regardless. User confirmed removal.
    **Fixed 2 more of the "9 unrelated" pre-existing failures incidentally** while running a broader safety sweep of tests referencing the touched CSS files: `mobileScrolling.contract.test.ts` (same `overflow-x`/`overflow-y` split pattern, plus a genuine `#root` scroll-ownership redesign) and `fullPlatformConsolidation.test.tsx` (a stale `PlatformEntryHub.jsx` path — the file was rebuilt to `.tsx` in an earlier cycle and this test's `readFileSync` call was never updated, so it failed at the file-read step before any assertion even ran).
    **Full re-verification after every change, not just once**: frontend `tsc --noEmit` 0 errors, `eslint src` (full tree) 0 errors, inline-style gate unaffected (512/690), all 29 touched CSS files individually confirmed brace-balanced, the full `src/styles` test directory (149/150 tests — the one remaining failure is a separate, narrower, unrelated finding flagged below, not touched this cycle), and a `src/pages/profile` + `src/components/account` + `src/components/profile` sweep (25/25 tests) as an extra safety net given how many profile-adjacent CSS files were restored.
    **One narrower finding flagged but not fixed this cycle**: `themeColorSystem.test.ts`'s "keeps key layout surfaces token-driven" assertion checks that `--app-accent-interactive` literally appears as a string in 5 specific files (`app-shell.css`, `CommandDashboard.css`, `Profile.css`, `DashboardVisualizations.css`, `ToolsOverview.css`) — confirmed via `git show` that none of these 5 files referenced this token even before the regression, so this is a separate, pre-existing, narrower gap unrelated to the monochrome bug just fixed. Left for a dedicated pass.
