17. **2026-07-14 — Cycle 45: the user supplied a full CareDroid Clinical Design System (CCDS) specification — an exact palette, role-accent color mapping, and a named application-shell architecture (ApplicationShell/ApplicationHeader/WorkspaceHeader/PageCommandBar/ApplicationSidebar/MobileNavigation/PageLayout) — and asked for a gap-check against the platform's full modernization brief.** Verified against actual code (not narrative) first: confirmed the exact palette didn't exist anywhere, confirmed `src/shell/` had zero consumers, confirmed role-accent colors didn't match the brief's mapping. Then closed 3 concrete items:
    **(1) CCDS palette adopted at the true root token layer** (`medical-color-layer.css`, not just the consumer-facing `theme-tokens.css` aliases). Root-caused a real, previously-undetected regression while doing this: `--medical-sky-300`–`700` (which `--medical-accent`/`--app-accent-interactive` resolve through) had been silently flattened from real blue to grey by the same commit (`752aff49`) Cycle 43 already investigated — but Cycle 43's fix only patched `theme-tokens.css`'s surface-level fallback text, never this deeper root, so the app's actual rendered accent stayed grey the whole time. Confirmed via `git diff 752aff49~1 752aff49`, not guessed. Also caught and fixed a real WCAG 2.4.11 gap before it shipped: a solid yellow (#FACC15) focus ring computed to ~1.5:1 contrast against white (verified via a real luminance calculation) — implemented as a 2-layer indicator instead (high-contrast blue solid ring + yellow glow), the same technique GOV.UK's design system uses for this exact problem.
    **(2) Role-accent colors corrected** to Nursing=Teal, EMS=a distinguishable green, Physician=a distinct Information Blue, Operations=Purple (new group, mapped to `edManager`). Caught and fixed my own mistake mid-pass: initially set Triage to the exact semantic-attention hex, then recognized this recreates the same "role accent collides with a real status color" problem this codebase's own `role-accent-theme.css` explicitly warns against — corrected to a distinguishable deep amber before it shipped.
    **(3) The inert `ApplicationShell` scaffold consolidated onto the real shell, not wired in as-is.** Investigated first rather than assuming the brief's literal instruction ("wire the scaffold into the router") was safe to follow verbatim: the scaffold was a rough mock — hardcoded fake "connected" health status, decorative-only search/notifications, no-op click handlers, a competing color-token system, heavy inline styles — that would have been a functional regression against the real, feature-complete `AppShell.tsx`/`Header.tsx`/`Sidebar.tsx`. Presented this fork to the user explicitly rather than guessing; user chose "rename/consolidate the real shell." `ApplicationShell`/`ApplicationHeader`/`ApplicationSidebar` are now thin re-exports of the real components (matching the existing `src/layouts/AppShell.tsx` canonical-path precedent); the fake `WorkspaceHeader`/`PageCommandBar`/`MobileNavigation` mocks were deleted (real `Sidebar.tsx` already has its own working mobile nav).
    **Full verification**: frontend + backend `tsc`/`eslint` 0 errors throughout; inline-style gate improved further to **409/690** (fake scaffold files were themselves inline-style-heavy); backend suite unaffected; **2 full frontend suite runs, both 837/837 files, 11567/11567 tests (100%)** — the cleanest full-suite result on record, zero regressions from any of this cycle's changes.
    Scorecard: Design language consistency 82→85, Module boundaries 48→51, Architecture 73→75, Frontend UX/UI 77→80, Accessibility 66→68 (the focus-ring fix). Overall readiness held flat at 94/95 — real, verified progress on 3 items, but the bulk of the user's full modernization brief (20-domain re-consolidation, full page completion, Playwright/visual-regression suites, a repository governance suite, the final Modernization Report) remains untouched, tracked explicitly rather than folded into an inflated headline number.

18. **2026-07-14 — Cycle 46: the user proposed a large "clinical workflow harmonization, OCR, and AI orchestration" program, premised on real OCR training infrastructure and broad AI-model integration already existing. Verified the premise via a dedicated Explore-agent audit before planning any work, and it was significantly wrong in the AI/OCR-optimistic direction — a genuine downward correction to two previously-recorded scores, not a regression.**
    **OCR: no real OCR/vision engine exists anywhere in this repo.** `createOcrProvider()` (`backend/src/modules/emergency-os/ocr-providers.ts`) only ever returns `MockOcrProvider`, whose `extract()` never reads the uploaded image data at all — it regex-parses `rawText`, which the frontend itself builds from `inferTextHintsFromFilename(file.name)` (the filename string, not file contents) plus manually-typed text. Zero OCR/vision packages exist in either `package.json`. What IS real: `OcrIntakeService` — a genuine, tested, enabled NestJS service with real job lifecycle/confidence-health/audit trail, a well-designed empty plug-in point for a real provider that was never added. The separate Mongoose-backed smart-intake session/MPI-matching subsystem is still real and substantive but still fully gated off, with a newly-noted route-collision risk if `ENABLE_MONGOOSE_EMERGENCY_OS` were ever flipped on (its legacy alias would collide with the primary NestJS controller's own intake routes). **User's explicit call**: document this honestly rather than build real OCR, since building a vision pipeline from scratch would be inventing new scope, directly against the brief's own instruction not to invent unrelated systems.
    **AI orchestration: corrected an overstated claim from Cycle 44.** `aiChiefOrchestrator.ts` genuinely is the single frontend AI entry point (confirmed zero competing LLM-calling paths) — that holds up. But of its "9 structured clinical AI domains," only `copilot_chat` actually calls a real LLM (Claude). The other 8 route to a deterministic rule/heuristic engine, self-labeled in code as `'careDroidAI-heuristic-node'` with an explicit "not foundation-model inference" comment — Cycle 44's re-investigation had correctly found real, tested infrastructure but missed this distinction. Also confirmed the safety posture is genuinely solid, not just claimed: zero auto-apply pattern anywhere, `requiresClinicianReview` hardcoded to the literal `true` in the response-provenance builder (not a settable boolean).
    **One concrete, bounded gap closed the same pass** (unlike OCR, this didn't require inventing anything — just wiring an already-declared field through): `AIAuditEvent.model` existed in the schema but was never populated at any of its 3 call sites, and no prompt-version field existed at all. Added `promptVersion: '1.0.0'` to all 8 registry prompts, and wired `model` through every audit-event builder — `aiChiefOrchestrator.ts`'s structured path now reads `response.provenance?.modelOrEngine` (correctly shows the heuristic-engine tag for 8 of 9 domains), its conversational path and `clinicalChatService.ts` now record `UNIFIED_AI_MODEL`, and `lib/ai/careDroidAI.ts`'s own audit logger does the same.
    **Confirmed, not rebuilt**: the "canonical workflow engine" the brief asked for already exists — `src/engine/journeyEngine.ts` is a real, enforced FSM (illegal transitions throw a typed error, not just get documented), with `unifiedPatientWorkflowModel.ts` layering a 10-step canonical journey on top and atomic audit-log + `workflowLogs` entries on every transition. **Confirmed as a real, still-open gap**: no unified event/pub-sub bus — the codebase's own comments admit *"no formal domain event bus... no EventEmitter2/@OnEvent usage was found"*; realtime/analytics/audit sync are wired via direct, centralized-but-separate method calls, not decoupled subscribers.
    **Verification**: frontend + backend `tsc`/`eslint` 0 errors; backend full suite **211/211 suites, 1725/1725 tests**; 5 targeted frontend test files, 25/25 passing (1 test's `vi.mock` needed `UNIFIED_AI_MODEL` added, since it now mocks a value the module actually exports).
    Scorecard: AI Chief orchestration depth 48→44, OCR & smart intake 55→30 (both genuine downward corrections to prior overstatement, not new regressions), Clinical workflow 69→61, Overall readiness 94→93. New memory file `project-ocr-ai-architecture-audit.md` records the full grounded findings for future sessions — don't re-describe CareDroid's AI as uniformly LLM-backed, and don't propose "improving the OCR model" without first re-reading that file.

19. **2026-07-14, later same day — Cycle 47: the user explicitly reversed Cycle 46's "document as gap" call and asked for real, self-hosted OCR** (clarified twice: not a from-scratch trained model — no dataset or GPU infra exists here for that — but a genuine, real, local OCR engine; confirmed `tesseract.js` satisfies "in house" since it runs entirely in-process with zero external calls).
    **Integrated `tesseract.js` at the exact plug-in point `createOcrProvider()` was already designed for** (`backend/src/modules/emergency-os/ocr-providers.ts`). New `TesseractOcrProvider` decodes the real uploaded image (`input.dataUrl`), runs genuine image-to-text recognition, and feeds the result into the same `parseIdArtifactText`/`parseClinicalArtifactText` structured-field parsers `MockOcrProvider` already used (extracted into a new shared `extractFieldsFromText()` helper) — so `OcrIntakeService`'s job lifecycle, confidence-health tracking, and audit trail all work unchanged regardless of which provider produced the result. Field confidence now blends Tesseract's real per-document confidence score with the existing field-type heuristic rather than a flat guess. PDFs are explicitly not rasterized this pass (Tesseract needs raster input) — falls back to any manually-supplied text with a clear warning rather than silently failing or crashing. `tesseract` is now the default provider (`mock` still available via `OCR_PROVIDER=mock`).
    **Proved it works rather than trusting that it compiled**: ran a manual proof-of-concept first (a synthesized image reading "PATIENT ID 42177" via Jimp, fed through a real Tesseract worker — extracted correctly at 95% confidence) before writing any production code. Then wrote a permanent test suite (`ocr-providers.spec.ts`) using real PNG fixtures (bitmap text rendered via Jimp, committed as binary files under `__fixtures__/` — generating them inline inside Jest failed because Jimp's font loader uses an internal dynamic `import()` Jest's default CJS transform can't execute, so fixtures are pre-rendered once and read as bytes instead) — genuinely extracts "Jordan"/"Rivera" from an image with zero `rawText` supplied, not asserted against a mock.
    **Found and fixed a real resource-leak bug the new dependency surfaced**: the lazily-created Tesseract worker (a live WASM/thread instance) was never released, hanging Jest indefinitely after tests completed. Added a `terminate()` method to `TesseractOcrProvider` (and an optional `terminate?()` on the shared `OcrProvider` interface), wired it into `OcrIntakeService` via NestJS's `OnModuleDestroy` lifecycle hook — this matters for production graceful shutdown, not just tests. Added `forceExit: true` to the backend Jest config as a backstop for a documented tesseract.js worker-thread quirk (a lingering handle even after `.terminate()` resolves) — confirmed this doesn't mask real failures, only prevents Jest from hanging on process exit.
    **Verification**: backend `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` 0 errors; full backend suite **212/212 suites, 1731/1731 tests** (up from 211/1725 — the new `ocr-providers.spec.ts` file); confirmed `npm install tesseract.js`/`jimp` introduced zero new vulnerabilities (the resulting `npm audit` findings are the same pre-existing, already-documented `@xenova/transformers`/protobufjs cluster — `tesseract.js` itself has zero transitive dependencies).
    Scorecard: OCR & smart intake 30→58, Backend reliability 65→66, Clinical workflow 61→66, Overall readiness 93→94.

20. **2026-07-14, later same day — Cycle 48: closed the `ai_queries` migration gap the RAG/AI-gateway audit found, and verifying it for real surfaced two more genuine pre-existing defects.**
    **Wrote `AddAIQueryTenantAndRoutingColumns1772600000000`**, adding the 10 columns `AIQuery` (the entity) had always declared but no migration ever created — `organizationId`, `workspaceId`, `assetId`, `agentId`, `modelClass`, `modelVersion`, `routingExpert`, `retrievalPolicy`, `requiresHumanReview`, `estimatedCost` — plus an index on `(organizationId, createdAt)` since that's exactly the filter `ai.service.ts` already queries by. `tsc`/`eslint` passing wasn't treated as sufficient proof: ran the **entire migration history forward from a genuinely empty database** (`DATABASE_CLIENT=sqlite`, scratch file, `npm run migration:run`) to prove it — not just my one new file, all of them, in real sequence.
    **That run failed on the migration before mine**, `CreateSentinelTables1772500000000` — real, pre-existing bug, not something this cycle introduced: `type: 'simple-json'` was copy-pasted from the entity decorator (`@Column({ type: 'simple-json' })`, a valid TypeORM *entity*-layer type) directly into raw migration DDL (`queryRunner.createTable`), where it isn't a real SQL type — SQLite's parser split it into `simple` `-` `json` and choked on the dash. This means a fresh SQLite-based checkout (the sandbox/dev-machine path, per `data-source.ts`'s `DATABASE_CLIENT=sqlite` branch) could never successfully run this project's own migrations end to end. Fixed by changing all 3 occurrences (`sentinel_units.metadata`, `sentinel_outbox.payload`, `sentinel_alarms.metadata`) to `type: 'text'` — matching exactly how this project's other JSON-ish columns (e.g. `ai_queries.metadata`) are already declared in their migrations, with the entity's own transformer handling serialize/deserialize regardless of the underlying physical column type.
    **A much bigger, separate finding surfaced testing the "backward" direction** (`npm run migration:revert`, per the "test forward, backward, forward again" requirement): reverting hit `SQLITE_ERROR: no such table: main.users`. Traced this to its root, not just the symptom — **no migration anywhere in this repository's history creates a `users` table**, despite the `User` entity declaring `@Entity('users')` and multiple tables (`ai_queries` among them) declaring real foreign keys to it. Confirmed via exhaustive search: zero migrations, zero `.sql` seed files, zero init/bootstrap scripts create it. SQLite's lenient FK-existence checking at `CREATE TABLE` time is why the **forward** run never surfaced this (it doesn't validate a referenced table exists unless enforcement triggers on an actual write/rebuild, which only happens during the ALTER-TABLE-via-rebuild the revert needed) — on Postgres, which the primary `app.module.ts` config actually targets for anything beyond bare local dev, this would very likely fail the same way. **This is a serious, separate, unresolved defect** — a fresh Postgres deployment following this project's own documented migration-based workflow (`migrationsRun: true`, `synchronize` confirmed `false`) would currently be unable to stand up its own `users` table. Deliberately did **not** attempt a `CreateUsers` migration in this same pass: getting a foundational auth table's schema right needs a careful, dedicated read of the full `User` entity and every other table with an FK to it, not an improvised addition at the tail end of an already-large cycle. Flagged as the top item for a focused follow-up session.
    **Verification**: full migration chain now runs forward from empty to completion without error (all tables including the fixed Sentinel ones and the new `ai_queries` columns); backend `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` 0 errors; full backend suite **212/212 suites, 1731/1731 tests**, unaffected.
    Scorecard: Backend reliability 66→68, Security & privacy 79→81 (closes a real tenant-scoping schema gap), Overall readiness 94→94 (held flat — the newly-discovered `users`-table gap is a bigger, still-open defect that offsets the fix just shipped).

21. **2026-07-14, later same day — Cycle 49: consolidated the 2 duplicate `AiGatewayService` implementations the RAG/AI-gateway audit found.** Confirmed `AiGatewayService` (`modules/ai/foundation/ai-gateway.service.ts`) was genuinely dead weight before removing it — registered in `AiModule`'s `providers`/`exports`, but grepping its class name found zero real callers of `createRunEnvelope()`/`toMetadata()` anywhere; every file importing its sibling types (`AiFoundationMetadata`, `AiRunEnvelope`) builds those objects independently via its own logic (e.g. `ai.service.ts`'s private `extractAiFoundationMetadata()`), never through this service. The real, actively-wired implementation (`modules/ai-gateway/ai-gateway.service.ts`'s `AIGatewayService`, imported by `ChatModule`/`ChatService`) is a strict superset — same shape plus `workspaceId`/`organizationId`, `clientRequestId`, real audit logging via `AuditService`, and richer routing metadata (`selectedExperts`, `routeScore`, `fallbackApplied`, etc.). Removed the dead one from `ai.module.ts`'s providers/exports/imports and `foundation/index.ts`'s re-export, then deleted `ai-gateway.service.ts` + its spec — kept `ai-foundation.types.ts` untouched since 4 other files genuinely depend on its types independent of the deleted service.
    **Verification**: backend `tsc --noEmit`/`eslint` 0 errors; full backend suite **211/211 suites, 1729/1729 tests** (down from 212/1731 — exactly the 1 suite / 2 tests belonging to the deleted dead-code spec file, confirmed not a regression).
    Scorecard: Architecture 75→77 (Module boundaries). Overall readiness held flat at 94/95, deliberately not bumped to the ceiling — the Cycle 48 `users`-table P0 remains a serious, unresolved defect, and one dead-code removal doesn't close that gap.

22. **2026-07-14, later same day — Cycle 50: closed the Cycle 48 P0, and the real scope turned out to be far larger than "write the missing `users` table migration."** Reading `User` and every entity with a foreign key to it (per the roadmap item's own instruction to do this carefully) surfaced the real picture: a repo-wide scan of all 65 `@Entity()`-declared tables against every migration's `createTable` calls found **46 tables — not 1 — with no migration anywhere**, including `users` itself, `organizations`/`workspaces` (the entire multi-tenancy foundation), `subscriptions`/`usage_events` (billing), `audit_logs`, `platform_assets`/`asset_packs`/`role_profiles` (the platform-assets suite), the full `product_catalog` suite, `notifications`/`notification_preferences`/`device_tokens`, 9 of 13 Sentinel EMS tables, and more. This confirms the app has only ever relied on `synchronize: true` for the vast majority of its schema — the 14 migrations that did exist were only ever written for the newest features added *after* someone noticed `synchronize` is (correctly) off for real Postgres deployments; nobody had gone back and written the foundational schema migrations for everything older.
    **Wrote 13 new migrations** (`1772700000000` through `1772701200000`), grouped by domain — core identity (`users`, `user_profiles`, `oauth_accounts`, `two_factor_auth`, `refresh_tokens`, `biometric_configs`), `audit_logs`, organizations/workspaces (6 tables), subscriptions/usage, platform-assets (4 tables), product-catalog (5 tables), notifications (3 tables), the 9 remaining Sentinel tables, analytics/automation-audit, clinical reference data (`drugs`/`protocols`), interoperability (discovered a 2nd hidden gap here: `integration-hub.entity.ts` declares 3 separate entities in one file, and the table-name extraction that found the other 45 gaps had only caught the first one per file — `integration_event_records` and `normalized_integration_events` were silently missing too until a targeted re-check caught it), personalization, and user-activity/preferences — each migration read directly from its entity file(s) (column types, defaults, enums, `@Index`/`@ManyToOne` relations) rather than guessed, matching this project's own established Table/TableColumn/TableIndex/TableForeignKey builder convention (not raw SQL, so the same migration works on both SQLite dev and Postgres prod).
    **Found and fixed a second, subtler pre-existing bug while verifying "backward"**: reverting the full chain back to empty (the same forward→backward→forward hygiene that found the original P0) failed with `SQLITE_ERROR: no such table: main.users` again — but for a *different* reason than Cycle 48's finding this time. `AddAIQueryTenantAndRoutingColumns1772600000000`'s `down()` calls `dropColumns()`, which SQLite implements via a full table-recreate that restates every live constraint on `ai_queries` — including its FK to `users` (added by the original `CreateAIQueryTable` migration). Since `CreateCoreIdentitySchema`'s `down()` (chronologically later, so it reverts *first*) drops `users` before this earlier migration's `down()` runs, the recreate step failed trying to reference an already-gone table. Fixed by having `AddAIQueryTenantAndRoutingColumns.down()` drop the FK before the column drops and only restore it afterward if `users` still exists (`queryRunner.hasTable('users')`) — correct for both a partial revert (FK restored, `users` still there) and a full revert-to-zero (skipped, since `CreateAIQueryTable`'s own `down()` tears down the whole table right after anyway).
    **Verified with the same rigor as Cycle 48, not just `tsc`/`eslint`**: ran the full 27-migration chain forward from a genuinely empty SQLite database (27/27 executed, 0 errors); confirmed all 65 entity-derived tables now exist with a scripted diff (0 missing, was 46); ran a full revert all the way to empty (27/27 reverted, 0 errors, including past the FK-ordering fix above) and forward again (27/27, 0 errors) — a complete round-trip, not just a one-way check. `tsc --noEmit` 0 errors, `eslint` 0 errors (38 Prettier formatting violations auto-fixed across the new migration files, then re-verified clean). Full backend suite **211/211 suites, 1729/1729 tests**, unaffected.
    **Flagging, not fixing, one thing noticed while reading these entities closely**: `UserProfile`'s `@BeforeInsert`/`@BeforeUpdate`/`@AfterLoad` PHI-encryption hooks (`encryptPhiBeforeInsert`, `encryptPhiBeforeUpdate`, `decryptPhiAfterLoad`) are empty method bodies with comments stating encryption "will be injected via service-level handler" — worth a dedicated verification pass to confirm PHI fields on `user_profiles` are actually encrypted somewhere at the service layer and not silently stored in plaintext, since the entity-level hooks that look like they'd do it are no-ops. Not investigated further this cycle (out of scope for a migration-writing pass) — added to the roadmap.
    Scorecard: Backend reliability 68→75 (closes the single largest reliability gap found this whole project — the persistence layer for 46 of 65 tables literally didn't exist outside of `synchronize` mode), Security & privacy 81→83 (every PHI/tenant-scoped table an entity declares now has a real, migration-backed schema, not just `ai_queries`). Overall readiness held flat at 94/95 — resolving the worst-known defect doesn't mean the platform is done; governance suite, Playwright/visual-regression suites, the unified event bus, 20-domain module reconsolidation, and the final Modernization Report are all still open.

23. **2026-07-15 — Cycle 51: added a real governance rule for the "ban ts-ignore/ts-nocheck/suppressions" roadmap item, then ran the platform's first-ever real circular-dependency and unused-export baselines.** Checked the actual blast radius before writing any rule: a repo-wide scan found **zero** `@ts-ignore`, **zero** `@ts-nocheck`, and **zero** blanket `// eslint-disable` comments anywhere in `src/`+`backend/src/` — only 5 scoped `eslint-disable-next-line` instances, all rule-specific and all carrying a justification comment (4 for a documented NestJS circular-module workaround, 1 for a Datadog dynamic require). Rather than write a rule with nothing to enforce against a codebase this clean, made the enforcement real going forward: added `@typescript-eslint/ban-ts-comment` (error) to both `eslint.config.ts` (frontend) and `backend/eslint.config.mjs`, banning `ts-ignore`/`ts-nocheck` outright and requiring a real 10+ character description on any future `ts-expect-error`; also enabled `linterOptions.reportUnusedDisableDirectives: 'error'` on both, so a `eslint-disable` comment that stops suppressing anything (e.g. after a refactor) now fails the lint gate instead of silently accumulating as dead weight. Verified zero new violations: frontend + backend `eslint` both still 0 errors with the new rules active.
    **Then ran a real circular-dependency scan** (`madge --circular`, first time this repo has had one) since this was long-standing scope on the "Extended baseline" roadmap item. **Frontend: 72 circular dependencies**, the large majority root-caused to one structural pattern — `store/emergencyStore.ts` (and a handful of "unified"-prefixed engines/services it pulls in: `unifiedApplicationKnowledgeGraphEngine`, `unifiedClinicalWorkflowOrchestrator`, `administrativeAutomationEngine`) sits at the center of a dense import web that loops back through itself via 8-14 hop chains touching `hospitalOperatingSystemService.ts` → `pages/emergency/emergencyRouteShared.tsx` → `components/PatientCard.tsx` and back out through a dozen different hooks/services. **Backend: 9 circular dependencies** — a much smaller, more tractable set; at least 2 (`ai.service.ts`↔`intent-classifier.service.ts`, mirrored at the module level) are already deliberately handled via NestJS's `forwardRef()` (confirmed live in `ai.module.ts`), so those aren't bugs, just a normal, working pattern for resolving circular DI in Nest — not investigated further. The other 7 (2 in `emergency-os`'s realtime/operational-intelligence services, 1 each in `types/emergency.ts`↔`lib/patient-orchestration/types.ts`, `platform-assets`↔`fleet` modules, and a 4-module chain through `chat`→`tool-calling`→`platform-systems`→`emergency-os`) are genuine, unexamined circular imports. **Deliberately not attempted to fix any of the 81 combined findings this cycle** — the frontend cluster in particular routes through `emergencyStore.ts`, the single most central piece of frontend state in the app; untangling it needs a dedicated architecture pass with its own test coverage plan, not a rushed drive-by that risks a subtle runtime regression in a clinical-safety application. Logged as a concrete, now-quantified roadmap item instead of a vague "some circular deps probably exist" guess.
    **Ran a real unused-export inventory** (`ts-prune`, since `knip`'s native binding was blocked by this sandbox's Application Control policy — the same class of block already documented for Playwright's Chromium binary) for the "dead-code inventory" roadmap item. **Frontend: ~1,953 exports `ts-prune` reports as fully unused** (4,164 total findings, 2,211 of which are flagged "used in module" — i.e. re-exported but not re-imported elsewhere, a much weaker signal). **Backend: ~702 fully-unused** (1,178 total, 476 "used in module"). **Explicitly treated as an inventory, not a deletion list** — `ts-prune` has well-documented false-positive modes (barrel-file re-exports, type-only exports consumed via `import type`, public component/API surfaces meant for external or future consumption, dynamic `import()` targets) that this pass did not individually triage; deleting from this raw list without per-file verification would risk exactly the kind of accidental-real-code-removal this project's own methodology has repeatedly warned against. This is now a real, measured number to start from for a future dedicated dead-code pass, not a guess.
    Scorecard: Architecture 77→78 (Type safety / maintainability — the ts-ignore/ts-nocheck ban is now enforced, not just aspirationally true). No other gauges moved: detection and inventory are prerequisites for remediation, not remediation itself.

24. **2026-07-15 — Cycle 52: closed the P2 flagged at the end of Cycle 50, `UserProfile`'s PHI-encryption hooks.** Traced the full path rather than assuming the worst from the placeholder comment: `UsersService.updateProfile()`'s field allow-list never included `dateOfBirthEncrypted`/`medicalHistoryEncrypted`/`allergiesEncrypted`/`medicationsEncrypted` — meaning no code path anywhere has ever written PHI to these columns, so this was never a live plaintext-storage leak (the columns are simply always null). Separately confirmed `User.emailEncrypted` (a sibling encrypted column on the `User` entity, not `UserProfile`) genuinely is encrypted via `AuthService.applyEmailEncryption()` calling the real `EncryptionService.encryptToBuffer()` (AES-256-GCM, scrypt key derivation) at 3 real call sites — that part of the encryption story is solid.
    **The real finding**: `UserProfile`'s `@BeforeInsert`/`@BeforeUpdate`/`@AfterLoad` lifecycle hooks were genuinely non-functional no-ops (empty bodies, and the `@AfterLoad` hook unconditionally reset 4 transient `*Decrypted` properties to `undefined` on every load) — dead code that could mislead a future developer into building a PHI-collection feature on the assumption that encryption already happens automatically via these hooks, when it does not. Removed the 3 hooks, the now-pointless transient properties, and the now-unused `BeforeInsert`/`BeforeUpdate`/`AfterLoad` imports; replaced with a direct code comment on the 4 PHI columns stating plainly that nothing populates them today and pointing at `AuthService.applyEmailEncryption()` as the correct pattern to follow if this capability gets built (explicit service-layer call, not an entity hook).
    **Verified**: confirmed zero test files anywhere reference the removed hooks/properties before deleting (only stale `dist/` build output did, which regenerates on next build). Backend `tsc --noEmit` 0 errors, `eslint` 0 errors, full suite **211/211 suites, 1729/1729 tests**, unaffected.
    Scorecard: Security & privacy 83→84 (PHI encryption reach — closes a real, if narrow, misleading-dead-code gap in HIPAA-adjacent entity code, and confirms the one PHI field that IS collected today — email — is genuinely encrypted, not just declared).

25. **2026-07-15, later same day — Cycle 53: investigated the "complete every remaining placeholder/unfinished page" roadmap item and found its premise doesn't hold against current code.** Searched for real placeholder markers (`Coming Soon`, `Under Construction`, `Not Yet Implemented`, `PlaceholderPage`, stub components) across all of `src/pages` — found exactly one genuine "not built yet" fallback: `Calculators.tsx`'s `default` switch case, which renders `ToolNotFound` for any calculator ID that doesn't have a dedicated form. This is not an unaddressed gap — it's the same, already-honestly-tracked 39-of-~219 clinical-tool-executor coverage line from earlier cycles, working exactly as designed (a labeled fallback, not a silent blank page).
    **Re-checked the "orphaned pages" list from the 2026-07-12 routes/navigation audit** (`project-routes-navigation-audit.md`) against current code: `simulation`, `laboratory`, `knowledge` (knowledge-graph), and `cosmos` — previously read as accidentally-orphaned — are now (or already were) explicitly listed in `CANONICAL_PILOT_EXTENSION_NAV_IDS` alongside `fleet`, the codebase's own established pattern for **deliberately** entitlement-gated SaaS surfaces, not accidental omissions. This is consistent with, and now further supported by, this program's own multi-tenancy/entitlement schema work (Cycle 50's `organization_entitlements`/`asset_packs` migrations) — these pages being gated behind an entitlement check is the intended product model, not a bug.
    **One genuinely inert, cosmetic finding, deliberately left alone**: `LEGACY_EMERGENCY_ROUTE_REDIRECTS` still contains `['/cosmos', CANONICAL_ROUTES.cosmosViewer]`, and `CANONICAL_ROUTES.cosmosViewer` is itself `'/cosmos'` — a self-referential redirect entry that resolves to a no-op (confirmed harmless: `/cosmos` is directly mounted as a real route, so this entry is simply never functionally exercised). 10 test files assert on this array's exact contents via `toEqual()`; removing one inert entry purely for tidiness would require updating all 10 for zero functional benefit to any real user path — not worth the churn, left as-is and noted here so a future session doesn't re-discover it as if it were new.
    **Conclusion**: no actionable "unfinished page" work exists to complete under this roadmap item as originally worded — the platform's actual gaps are entitlement-gating decisions (working as designed) and the already-tracked clinical-tool-executor ceiling (39/~219, itself its own separate, already-closed roadmap item). Marking this item resolved-by-investigation rather than leaving it as a perpetually-open "todo" describing work that doesn't exist.

26. **2026-07-15, later same day — Cycle 54: added real static accessibility linting (`eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y`) — a tool this repo never had — and fixed every one of the 87 real violations it found across 25 files, each with the correct targeted repair rather than a blanket suppress.** Also fixed an incidentally-discovered `npm audit` finding (esbuild 0.27.7, a Windows dev-server-only arbitrary-file-read advisory, transitively pulled in by Vite; patched via `npm audit fix` to 0.28.1, non-breaking, back to 0 vulnerabilities).
    **Genuine bugs fixed, not just lint noise**: (a) a clickable analytics dashboard card (`PredictiveAnalyticsDashboard.tsx`) had zero keyboard support at all — `cursor: pointer` + `onClick` on a plain `<div>` with no `role`, `tabIndex`, or `onKeyDown` — keyboard-only users could never expand it; added real `role="button"`/`tabIndex={0}`/Enter-Space handling. (b) `PatientCard.tsx` used `<article role="button">` — a real WAI-ARIA anti-pattern (overriding a semantically-rich element's own native role) — swapped to a plain `<div>` (which has no competing implicit semantics) after confirming zero CSS depends on the `article` tag itself. (c) `WhiteboardPatientRowHeader.tsx` put `aria-sort` on a `<button>`, where it's not a valid attribute (only `columnheader`/`rowheader` support it) — fixed by giving the button an explicit `role="columnheader"` override rather than restructuring the grid-based layout (confirmed native click/keyboard behavior is preserved regardless of the ARIA role attribute). (d) 3 `autoFocus` usages on native inputs (`WorkloadBalancePanel.tsx`, `PediatricDrugCalc.tsx`, `SelfCheckin.tsx`) were converted to ref-based focus-on-mount — and a real bug was caught and avoided while doing this: a naive inline `ref={(el) => el?.focus()}` re-fires on every parent re-render (React detaches/reattaches a ref whenever its function identity changes, and an inline arrow function gets a new identity every render), which would have **stolen focus back from whatever field the user had already tabbed to on every keystroke elsewhere in the same form** — fixed by using a stable `useCallback`/`useRef`-based ref identity instead, verified this doesn't refire on re-render. (e) `Sidebar.tsx` had 5 instances of a plain-text nav label mistakenly wrapped in `<label>` (a form-control tag) instead of this exact file's own established `<span className="sidebar-nav-item__label">` convention used everywhere else — only 2 of the 5 were even flagged by the linter, but all 5 were the identical bug, so all 5 were fixed for consistency, not just the flagged ones. (f) `user_profiles`... *(see Cycle 52 for that one)*.
    **Real false positives correctly identified and suppressed with justification, not silently worked around**: modal-backdrop click-outside-to-dismiss patterns (5 files) where a real, separate, keyboard-accessible Close button already exists; `aria-selected`/`aria-checked` set via a conditional prop-spread the static linter can't trace (4 files); a `<label>` correctly wrapping its `<input>` where the linter can't verify a JSX expression renders real text (3 files); a `<ul role="list">` that's a deliberate Safari/VoiceOver fix for `list-style: none` stripping implicit list semantics (confirmed via the component's own CSS before keeping it); a `<form onKeyDown>` for Escape/Enter shortcuts (forms are genuinely interactive, just not on this rule's default allow-list); and `CopilotMessage`'s `role` prop, which is a custom `'ai' | 'user'` component prop (confirmed via its type definition), not a DOM ARIA attribute at all.
    **Two real, functionally-unused findings closed correctly for their actual situation**: `RoleDashboardPanel.tsx`'s `tabIndex={0}` on a purely-decorative loading-state `<article>` with no interactive behavior was removed outright (making an inert element focusable is itself an accessibility anti-pattern — needless Tab-stop noise); `surfaces/Card.tsx`'s conditional `tabIndex={interactive ? 0 : undefined}` was paired with a matching conditional `role="button"` — confirmed via a repo-wide grep that this component has **zero current importers** (fully orphaned), so this is correctness-for-the-future rather than a live-bug fix.
    **Verification**: frontend `eslint src` — 0 errors (was 87). Frontend `tsc --noEmit` — 0 errors. `npm audit` — 0 vulnerabilities (was 1, unrelated pre-existing Vite/esbuild transitive advisory, fixed as a bonus). Full frontend vitest suite and full backend suite re-run clean (see Methodology).
    Scorecard: Accessibility 68→76 (87 real, fixed violations plus a new permanent governance rule catching future regressions — this is static-analysis coverage, not a live-browser axe-core/WCAG certification, which remains a separate open item). Architecture 78→79 (Type safety / maintainability — a second real governance rule now enforced with zero prior violations, extending Cycle 51's ts-ignore/ts-nocheck ban).

28. **2026-07-15, later same day — Cycle 56: re-audited the "resolve the 7 genuine backend circular dependencies" roadmap item (P2, first flagged Cycle 51) against current source, in the same "check the record against reality" spirit as the `AiGatewayService` catch above.** Re-ran `npx madge --circular --extensions ts src` from `backend/`: still 9 flagged cycles, but the actual composition has shifted since Cycle 51 — 2 of today's 9 (`src/types/emergency.ts`↔`src/types/patientDocumentArtifact.ts`, and `ai.service.ts`↔`intent-classifier.service.ts` at the constructor-injection level) don't appear in the original description at all, while its "2 of 9 are intentional forwardRef patterns" undercounts what's actually there now. Read every one of the 9 flagged file pairs directly rather than trusting the one-line madge summary: (a) `src/types/emergency.ts`↔`lib/patient-orchestration/types.ts` and `src/types/emergency.ts`↔`src/types/patientDocumentArtifact.ts` are both pure `import type` cross-references (`TriageAssistEnvelope`/`Priority`/`ISODateString`) — erased entirely at compile, zero runtime coupling, a normal pattern for mutually-referential TS interfaces; (b) `ai.service.ts`↔`intent-classifier.service.ts` uses `@Inject(forwardRef(() => IntentClassifierService))` at the constructor-injection level (`ai.service.ts:76-77`), and the matching module pair (`ai.module.ts`↔`intent-classifier.module.ts`) mirrors it with `forwardRef()` on both sides; (c) `platform-assets.module.ts`↔`fleet.module.ts` also uses `forwardRef()` on both sides; (d) the 4-module `chat`→`tool-calling`→`platform-systems`→`emergency-os` chain closes via `forwardRef(() => ChatModule)` on the `emergency-os.module.ts` side (NestJS only needs one side of a mutual-module pair to use it); (e) the 3-cycle `emergency-os` service triangle (`emergency-os.services.ts`/`emergency-realtime.service.ts`/`emergency-os.operational-intelligence.service.ts`) breaks the cycle at runtime via a deliberate lazy `require()` + `ModuleRef.get()` pattern inside `emergency-realtime.service.ts`, already carrying its own explanatory comment (`// Deferred require avoids circular Nest module graph at bootstrap.`) plus an `eslint-disable` justification — a documented workaround, not an accidental one. `madge` flags all 9 regardless, since it's a static import-graph tool with no awareness of `import type` erasure or NestJS DI semantics. Net finding: 0 of the 9 need a code fix. No source touched this cycle — this is a roadmap-accuracy correction, not a new capability, so no gauge was bumped for it.

30. **2026-07-15, later same day — Cycle 58: closed the RAG tenant-scoping gap the RAG/AI-gateway audit had confirmed but left unfixed (roadmap since Cycle 51).** Dispatched a read-only research pass first to establish the real blast radius before writing any code: `IVectorDatabase`'s `VectorQueryOptions.filter` and `PineconeService.buildFilter()` had zero `organizationId`/tenant concept anywhere, and neither did `ChunkMetadata` or the ingestion path (`bootstrapStarterCorpus()` tags documents with `organization: 'CareDroid Reference Corpus'` — a citation/publisher label, not a tenant id). Confirmed this was **not yet exploitable**: the only corpus ever ingested today is the shared public guideline set, and no endpoint exposes org-specific document ingestion. But confirmed the caller's real `organizationId` (`req.user.profile.organizationId`, loaded by `JwtStrategy` via the `profile` relation) was available at every retrieval call site and simply never read — so the instant a future feature adds tenant-specific document ingestion into this same shared index/namespace, Org A's query would retrieve Org B's chunks with zero barrier.
    **Fix**: added `organizationId` to `ChunkMetadata` (`rag-context.dto.ts`), `MedicalSource` (`medical-source.dto.ts`), and `RAGRetrievalOptions`. `RAGService.ingest()` now stamps every chunk with `enrichedSource.organizationId || RAG_GLOBAL_ORG_SCOPE` (a new exported sentinel) rather than leaving it unset — the shared starter corpus is automatically tagged global since its source objects never set `organizationId`. `buildRetrievalFilter()` now adds `filter.organizationId = [options.organizationId, RAG_GLOBAL_ORG_SCOPE]` whenever a caller supplies one, scoping results to the caller's own tenant plus the shared corpus; callers without tenant context get no filter at all, preserving existing unscoped behavior rather than breaking them. Threaded the real `organizationId` through all 3 confirmed `ragService.retrieve()` call sites in the whole backend (grepped repo-wide to be sure): `chat.controller.ts`'s `sendMessage` → `ChatService.processMessage()` (new trailing parameter, matching the method's existing positional-args style) → both its general-response branch and its `handleMedicalReference()` helper; and `clinical-intelligence.controller.ts`'s `queryGuidelineEvidence` → `ClinicalIntelligenceService.queryGuidelineEvidence()`. Confirmed `RetrievalService.cacheKey()` already hashes the full filter object it's given, so folding `organizationId` into that same filter closes the cross-tenant cache-poisoning risk for free — no separate cache-key change was needed.
    **Found and closed a real test-coverage gap along the way**: `RAGService` — the module's central orchestrator — had zero unit tests of its own (only its collaborators `citation`/`clinical-context`/`embedding`/`retrieval` services had specs). Wrote `rag.service.spec.ts` (4 tests) proving, against the real `ingest()`/`retrieve()` code paths (not just asserting the type compiles): a caller-scoped request's filter includes `[callerOrgId, RAG_GLOBAL_ORG_SCOPE]`; an unscoped caller's filter has no `organizationId` key at all; a tenant-owned document's chunks are all tagged with that tenant's id; and a public document's chunks are all tagged with the global sentinel.
    **Verification**: backend `tsc --noEmit` — 0 errors. `eslint` — 1 Prettier formatting issue on first pass, auto-fixed, 0 errors after. `nest build` — succeeds. Full backend suite — **215/215 suites, 1770/1770 tests** (up from 214/1766 — the 4 new tests, zero regressions across `chat`, `clinical-intelligence`, and `rag` modules).
    Scorecard: Security & privacy 84→86 (new "Multi-tenant data isolation" row) — the largest single-item jump of this cycle, since this closes a roadmap item explicitly confirmed as "a real, substantiated gap" rather than a documentation correction or an already-safe pattern.

31. **2026-07-15, later same day — Cycle 59: re-checked the "Playwright/axe-core suite... this sandbox cannot spawn a browser process" roadmap line against reality, and found it stale — the exact kind of drift the last two cycles' scorecard-comparison work has been catching.** A prior cycle (Cycle 7, Reception audit) had already found that this sandbox's Application Control policy blocks the *downloaded* Playwright Chromium but not the system's already-installed, already-trusted Edge binary; that workaround was used once, ad hoc, and never made reusable. Re-tested it fresh: `playwright-core`'s `chromium.launch({ executablePath: <system Edge> })` still works today. Added a small, env-var-gated escape hatch to `playwright.a11y.config.ts` (`launchOptions.executablePath` from `QA_CHROMIUM_EXECUTABLE`, no effect unless set) so this doesn't need rediscovering next time, then ran the app's own already-written `e2e/a11y.spec.mjs` — 8 representative pages, real `@axe-core/playwright` scan, WCAG 2.1 A/AA tags — for the first real time in this project's history rather than leaving it "written and waiting."
    **Found 259 raw color-contrast violation mentions across the 8 pages, all one rule id (`color-contrast`), overwhelmingly concentrated in the persistent sidebar/header chrome present on every page** (nav item/group labels, brand tag, header clock, account menu) — a systemic token problem, not 8 separate per-page bugs. Traced to source: `--medical-ink-muted` (`#627d98`) measured 4.28:1 against white (needs 4.5) and `--medical-ink-subtle` (`#94a3b8`) measured 2.45–2.56:1 — both used app-wide for de-emphasized text. Fixed at the token root (`medical-color-layer.css`'s `:root` block only, not the separately-scoped `[data-theme="dark"]` block, which is unreachable — the app hardcodes light theme) by re-pointing both to existing, already-defined `--medical-slate-500`/`-600` steps (`#64748b`/`#475569`, 4.76:1/7.58:1) rather than inventing new colors — "subtle" ends up darker than "muted" as an unavoidable consequence of both needing to stay real, AA-legible text on white; visual hierarchy between them now has to come from type treatment (size/weight/tracking) at each call site, not raw lightness. Also found and fixed 2 files with the exact same bad grey hardcoded as a literal (bypassing the token system entirely): `CriticalChecklist.css` and `AiCommandCenterDashboard.css`, both re-pointed to `var(--medical-ink-subtle)`.
    **Re-ran the same real-browser scan to verify, not just assert, the fix**: raw violation-node mentions across all 8 pages dropped from 259 to ~48 (81%), and distinct offending color/background pairs dropped from 13 to 6. Two large-text violations (`#22c55e`/`#f59e0b` at 24px, unrelated to the ink tokens) present in the baseline scan did not reproduce in the post-fix re-scan — not claiming these as fixed since nothing in this cycle's changes touched them; flagged as possibly data/timing-dependent and left for a dedicated look. Honestly still open, not fixed this cycle: 4 combos of `--medical-ink-muted` on specific tinted (not pure-white) component backgrounds still fall just short of 4.5:1, one more not-yet-traced `#94a3b8` instance on a `#f8fafc` background, and a white-on-amber alarm badge (`OperationalAlarmDock.css`, `#ffffff` on `#d97706` = 3.18:1) — all smaller, more scattered, component-specific fixes better done as their own pass than folded into this one.
    **Found and fixed a real bug in the test file's own reporting while doing this**: `a11y.spec.mjs` collected all 8 pages' results into one module-level array with a single `test.afterAll()` writing one shared JSON file — but `fullyParallel: true` (plus retries) runs this file's tests across multiple worker processes, each with its own module instance, so the shared array only ever held whichever worker's subset finished last, and the committed `qa/a11y-results.json` had silently been capturing just 1 of 8 pages for who knows how long. Rewrote it to write one file per page immediately inside each test body (`qa/a11y-results/{pageId}.json`) — race-free regardless of worker/retry topology, verified by re-running with the default 2-worker config and confirming all 8 files write correctly. Gitignored the per-page output directory and the two now-obsolete shared report files (`qa/a11y-results.json`, `qa/playwright-a11y-report.json`), matching this repo's existing convention for generated QA artifacts.
    **Verification**: frontend `tsc --noEmit` — 0 errors. `eslint` — 0 errors on the touched `.mjs`/`.ts`/`.css` files. Targeted vitest run of every test file that could plausibly assert on the changed tokens/components (`CriticalChecklist.test.tsx`, `semanticColorSystem.test.ts`, `medicalThemeAudit.test.ts`, `themeColorSystem.test.ts`, `Sidebar.test.tsx`, `SidebarChromeControls.test.tsx`, `SidebarNotificationPanel.test.tsx`) — **37/37 passing**, zero regressions. Full frontend suite not re-run this cycle (CSS-only + one test-infra file changed; the targeted slice covers every consumer a repo-wide grep for the changed token names and component names could find).
    Scorecard: Accessibility 76→80 (+4) — the first real live-browser WCAG evidence this project has ever had, a systemic token-level fix (not a one-page patch) with a measured, honest before/after, plus a corrected stale roadmap claim in the same "verify before trusting the last note" spirit as Cycles 55/56.

32. **[⚠ Corrected in Cycle 61, item 33: the specific numbers below are invalid — the measurement silently hit a leftover dev-mode Vite server, not the production build as claimed. The suite, the approach, and the "TBT is the problem" conclusion all survived re-verification against a real production server; the evidence numbers did not. Kept unedited below for the honest record.]** **2026-07-15, later same day — Cycle 60: extended Cycle 59's system-Edge workaround to Performance, the single biggest remaining gauge gap (57/90, a 33-point deficit no other category came close to).** The roadmap had bundled "Playwright/axe-core suite and Lighthouse profiling" under one line item and one blocker; Cycle 59 already proved the "sandbox can't spawn a browser" premise stale for the a11y half. This cycle reused the exact same `playwright-core` + system-Edge launch path for the performance half, since installing and wiring up the full `lighthouse` package (a much heavier dependency — bundled Chrome launcher, its own audit pipeline) wasn't necessary when the browser's own native `PerformanceObserver` APIs give the same core metrics directly.
    **Built the real production bundle first** (`npm run build`) and measured against `vite preview`, not the dev server — dev-mode Vite serves unbundled ES modules with no minification and adds HMR client overhead, so its numbers would have been meaningless noise, not a real baseline. Wrote `e2e/performance.spec.mjs` (mirroring the a11y suite's page list and QA auth/network-stub helpers for a directly comparable baseline) capturing Navigation/Paint Timing, LCP and CLS via `PerformanceObserver({buffered: true})`, and a long-task-derived Total Blocking Time approximation, across all 8 pages.
    **Findings, not yet fixes**: FCP/LCP are mostly "good" per Core Web Vitals thresholds (1.2–1.8s on 6 of 8 pages), but **Total Blocking Time is "needs improvement" to "poor" on every single page** (219–659ms against Lighthouse's 200ms "good" ceiling) — `clinical-alerts` is the worst outlier at 659ms TBT, 13 long tasks, and a 3644ms LCP (crossing into "needs improvement" territory, unlike its peers). Every page also cold-loads **~250 resources and ~10.5 MB of transfer**, consistent with — and now a hard, browser-measured number behind — the already-tracked "Bundle size & code-splitting: WARN 63" row. Deliberately did **not** invent pass/fail thresholds for this first run: a single local sample on a shared dev machine isn't a stable enough basis to gate on, so the suite reports metrics without hard assertions, same discipline as introducing any new, unvalidated measurement.
    **Verification**: `eslint` — 0 errors on both new files. Ran the suite twice (once to validate the metric-capture logic, once after adding total-resource-transfer collection) — 8/8 passing both times, numbers consistent run-to-run within normal variance.
    Scorecard: Performance 57→59 (+2) — a real, honest baseline now exists where there was none, but this is explicitly measurement, not optimization; no code changed this cycle, so the bump reflects new capability and new evidence, not improved speed. See the new P2 roadmap row for the TBT/page-weight fix this surfaced.

33. **2026-07-15, later same day — Cycle 61: root-caused the Cycle 60 TBT finding, caught and corrected Cycle 60's own invalid measurement in the process, and shipped two real, measured performance fixes.**
    **The self-caught measurement error first.** To find *which* code was blocking the main thread, wrote a CDP sampling profiler over the same system-Edge path (`Profiler.start/stop` via Playwright's `newCDPSession`, aggregating V8 self-time per URL/function — now committed as permanent tooling, `scripts/perf-profile.mjs`). The very first profile came back with raw `/src/main.tsx` and `/node_modules/.vite/deps/*` URLs — dev-server artifacts that cannot exist in a production bundle. Cycle 60's "production" measurement had silently hit a **leftover dev-mode Vite server**: an earlier session's dev stack was still listening on the target port, and `playwright.performance.config.ts`'s `reuseExistingServer: !process.env.CI` reused it instead of starting `vite preview`. This also explains the impossible result nobody questioned at the time — all 8 pages reporting byte-identical ~250 resources/~10.5MB. Item 32 is annotated, not rewritten. Re-measured against a genuinely isolated `vite preview` on its own port, after verifying via `curl` that it serves hashed `dist/assets/*` files. **The true production baseline is materially different and mostly better than Cycle 60 believed**: LCP "good" on 8/8 pages (1.0–2.1s), CLS "good" on 8/8 (≤0.047), per-page transfer ~1.6–2.1MB across 81–113 resources — varying by route, as a real code-split build should. What survives the correction: **TBT genuinely is "needs improvement"-to-"poor" everywhere** (per-page means 440–1171ms vs. the 200ms "good" ceiling). Cycle 60's conclusion stands; its evidence didn't.
    **Fix 1 — profiled, then A/B-verified: `routePatternMatches` regex recompilation.** The CPU profile put it among the hottest application functions: it rebuilt and recompiled a `RegExp` from scratch on every dynamic-route comparison, and `getRouteByPath`/`getRouteAccess` invoke it per route record on every nav-item render. Added a compiled-pattern memo cache in `src/config/routes.config.ts` — safe because `CANONICAL_ROUTE_MAP` is a fixed, frozen, module-load-time array, so the pattern set is bounded and immutable. Verified: frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, 43/43 route/navigation tests green (`canonicalAccess`, `unified-navigation.config`, `canonicalRouteRedirects`), and a real A/B against the isolated production server (stash the fix → rebuild → 2 measured runs; restore → rebuild → 2 runs): mean TBT 808→692ms (−14%), improved on 7 of 8 pages — honestly noted as high-variance at n=2 per side, so the claim is "directionally consistent gain," not a precise percentage.
    **Fix 2 — the structural one: every page was fetching and executing the entire clinical-calculator catalog at startup.** The clean baseline showed `dist/index.html` module-preloading all 11 specialty calculator chunks plus a 1,226KB catalog chunk on every route. Traced it three ways before touching anything: a BFS static-import tracer proved `pages/tools/Calculators.tsx` is reachable *only* through lazy routes at module level; a sourcemap build proved the `manualChunks` rule pinning it to a named `'calculators'` chunk had made Rollup fuse **~200 shared startup modules** (`apiClient`, `UserContext`, env/auth config, logger…) into that same chunk; and the dist chunk-import headers proved the entry therefore statically imported it — executing the whole catalog plus every specialty chunk on all ~150 routes to get at its own infrastructure. Removed that one `manualChunks` rule (hazard documented in a comment in `vite.config.ts`). Verified at all three levels post-fix: startup preloads dropped from ~20 JS files (including all 12 calculator chunks) to **8 files / 3.63MB uncompressed with zero calculator chunks**; `ToolsOverview`'s lazy chunk correctly absorbed the page code (121→330KB); and behaviorally across 5 measurement runs vs. the 2 pre-fix runs: **LCP improved on 7 of 8 pages** (hospital-map ~1462→~1100ms, clinical-alerts ~1408→~1116ms, tools-overview ~1706→~1444ms), TBT improved on 6 of 8 (dashboard ~1171→~1022ms, devices ~502→~440ms), roughly flat on `assistant`.
    **Honest regression, by design**: the calculators page itself now pays its own load cost on entry instead of having it pre-paid by every other route — its TBT went bimodal (0.6s–3.5s across the 5 runs). Architecturally correct (pay-for-what-you-use), but flagged as the next optimization: `Calculators.tsx` statically imports all specialty families, so lazy-loading them per family inside the hub is the follow-up. Second thread deliberately left unpulled: `analytics` + `vendor-charts` (~660KB) are still eagerly preloaded and show the same fusion signature — scoped to the roadmap rather than rushed into this cycle.
    Scorecard: Performance 59→63 (+4) — two real, verified optimizations plus a corrected (and mostly better-than-believed) baseline; held well short of target because TBT remains above the 200ms "good" ceiling on every page, the calculator hub's own load is now the worst outlier, and coverage is still 8 representative pages. Bundle size & code-splitting WARN 63→68; Runtime performance WARN 55→60. Overall readiness held at 94/95.

34. **2026-07-15, later same day — Cycle 62: full Monday-readiness verification audit** against the recurring team-meeting brief (AI/API, RAG, database/isolation/CI, Copilot, EMS-handoff workstreams). Audit-only — no gauge changes; full detail in `docs/MONDAY_AI_RAG_READINESS_REPORT.md`. Every classification backed by a command run or file:line read this same day, nothing inferred. Fresh baseline: backend 215/215 suites (1,770 tests), both trees `tsc`/`eslint` 0/0, migrations 27/27 from a truly empty database (82 tables, 0 pending on re-run), frontend 834/837 full-parallel (implicated file passes 7/7 in isolation; documented contention signature). | Backend reliability & ops | GOOD 85 (was 83) | **New this cycle (96): found and fixed a real, currently-live defect — the app could not cold-boot against a fresh SQLite database at all.** Investigating the long-neglected "Deployment & observability" row (see that row below) led to a real live-boot test: `PORT=... SQLITE_PATH=<fresh-file> DATABASE_CLIENT=sqlite node dist/backend/src/main.js` against an empty database crashed on every retry with `QueryFailedError: SQLITE_ERROR: AUTOINCREMENT is only allowed on an INTEGER PRIMARY KEY`. Root-caused, not guessed: `backend/src/app.module.ts` hardcodes `synchronize: true` whenever `DATABASE_CLIENT=sqlite` (unconditional, not gated on `NODE_ENV` the way the Postgres branch is) — meaning SQLite boot has never actually used the `migrationsRun: true` path this project's own migration-verification discipline exercises every cycle; it silently derives schema straight from entity decorators instead. `CigOutboxEntity` (`cig_outbox`, from the Cycle 75-76 CIG work) declares its primary key `@PrimaryGeneratedColumn({ type: 'bigint' })`, but its own migration (`1772701500000-CreateCigOperationalGraph.ts`) declares the same column `type: 'integer'` — a real drift between the migration (correct, SQLite-safe, the source of truth for every `migration:run` check this project has ever done) and the entity decorator (Postgres-oriented, SQLite-incompatible via `synchronize`). Fixed by changing the entity to `type: 'integer'`, matching its own migration exactly; confirmed no code reads the outbox row's `id` in a way that depended on the old (already-inaccurate) `bigint` typing. **Verified live, not just re-compiled**: rebuilt, booted fresh against a brand-new empty SQLite file — `/health/live` now returns `200` instead of an infinite retry loop. Full regression re-run to catch any ripple effect from touching a bootstrap-adjacent file: **full backend suite 244/244 suites, 1,910/1,910 tests**; CIG module suite (5 files/12 tests) and `app.module.spec.ts` individually re-confirmed; migration round trip re-verified forward→revert→forward, **32/32 from empty**; `tsc`/eslint 0/0 (including one pre-existing, not mine, prettier violation on the same entity file, confirmed via stash A/B before fixing). **This means every cycle's own "migration round trip" claim for SQLite was accurate on its own terms (the migration CLI has always used real migrations) — but the live app's actual dev-mode boot behavior never matched it**, a real, previously-invisible gap between what this project verifies and what it runs. **New this cycle (92): closed the durability gap Cycle 91 found and named honestly rather than glossed over** — EMS handoff completion (`POST /api/emergency/ems/handoff`) and every other caller of the shared `WorkflowActionLogService` (~18 call sites: journey-state changes, clinician assignment, reassessment, boarding, referrals, escalations, patient notes...) persisted to an in-memory array only, capped at 500 entries, lost on restart. Same class of gap Cycle 77 closed for AI action proposals, closed the same way: a write-through journal (`workflow_action_logs` table, migration `1772701800000`, chain now **32/32 from empty**, verified forward→revert→forward) behind the unchanged synchronous `record()`/`listLogs()` contract — `onModuleInit()` rehydrates the newest `WORKFLOW_LOG_BUFFER_LIMIT` (500) rows on restart, matching the in-memory eviction policy so a restart can't resurrect entries the buffer would already have evicted; journal writes are fire-and-forget (`.catch(() => undefined)`), so a DB failure never breaks the synchronous logging contract any caller (including the EMS handoff endpoint) depends on. Fixing this in the shared service rather than EMS-specifically closes the gap for all ~18 workflow-log call sites at once, not just the one Cycle 91 happened to name. 7 new tests (write-through shape, restart rehydration, buffer-limit-matching rehydration query, journal-write-failure resilience, journal-unavailable-at-startup resilience, corrupt-payload-skipped, in-memory-only mode without a repository) + full `emergency-os` module regression re-run clean, **13 suites / 66 tests**, confirming zero breakage of the pre-existing patient/EMS/journey/collaboration workflow. `tsc`/eslint 0/0 on all touched and new files (including auto-fixing 3 pre-existing, not mine, prettier violations in `emergency-os.services.ts` since the file was already being touched, verified pre-existing via stash A/B). **New this cycle (89): closed the hash-chain-audit half of Cycle 77's own "honest remainder"** ("the pre-consolidation design's hash-chain audit per transition not yet re-added"). New `ai_action_proposal_audit_entries` table (migration `1772701700000`, chain now **31/31 from empty**, verified with a full forward→revert→forward round trip, not a one-way check) + `AIActionProposalAuditEntry` entity: one append-only row per proposal transition (including creation and lazy auto-expiry), each row's `entryHash` covering its own fields plus the *previous* row's hash — editing or removing a stored row breaks the chain from that point forward, detectable without a separate signing key. `verifyAuditChain()` recomputes the chain via the exact same pure hash function the writer uses (so a verifier bug can't silently diverge from what was written) and returns the first broken sequence index; proven with a real tamper test — corrupt one field on one durable row, restart the service, rehydrate, and `verifyAuditChain` correctly reports `valid: false` at that exact index. New `GET /api/ai/proposals/:proposalId/audit` route exposes the trail + verification. 9 new tests (genesis entry shape, per-transition chaining, tamper detection after simulated restart, restart-survival with no tampering, independent per-proposal chains, auto-expiry is itself audited, in-memory-only mode without a repository, journal-failure resilience, empty/unknown-proposal handling) — full `ai` module suite re-run **55/55**, `tsc`/eslint 0/0. **Honest remainder, unchanged**: the single-node/multi-instance read-model consistency question Cycle 77 also flagged is a separate, larger concern (horizontal scaling of the in-process store) and wasn't touched this cycle. New: `/health/live` fast liveness route (no dependency fan-out) for dev-stack boot polling, and every `/health` component check now runs under a `withTimeout()` guard so one hung dependency probe can no longer stall the whole response (carried forward). **New this cycle (50):** closed the Cycle 48 P0 in full — 46 of 65 entity-declared tables (not just `users`) had no migration anywhere and existed only under `synchronize: true`, meaning a real Postgres deployment following this project's own `migrationsRun: true` workflow could never have stood up the large majority of its own schema. Wrote 13 domain-grouped migrations covering every gap (core identity, organizations/workspaces, subscriptions, platform-assets, product-catalog, notifications, 9 remaining Sentinel tables, analytics/automation-audit, clinical reference data, interoperability, personalization, user-activity/preferences), plus fixed a second, subtler bug found while verifying: an SQLite FK-revert-ordering hazard in `AddAIQueryTenantAndRoutingColumns`'s `down()` (recreate-table semantics restating a FK to an already-reverted `users` table). Verified with a full round trip, not a one-way check: 27/27 migrations forward from empty, all 65 tables confirmed present via a scripted diff (0 missing), 27/27 reverted all the way back to empty, 27/27 forward again — all 0 errors. |

| Runtime performance (live-browser measurement) | **WARN 64 (was 60)** | **Corrected + improved Cycle 61; re-measured fresh Cycle 86 for the first time since both the analytics/vendor-charts fusion (Cy83) and the calculator-hub lazy-loading confirmation (Cy85) landed.** Cycle 60's first-ever measurement was real but invalid (stale dev server); corrected Cy61 baseline: LCP good 8/8, TBT 440–1171ms per-page means. **Cycle 86 baseline — 3 clean runs, each on a freshly-verified port-clear isolated `vite preview` (no reused-server contamination), against a build with zero production-code changes this cycle**: 7 of 8 pages now mean **268–614ms TBT** (assistant 558, calculators-hub 547, clinical-alerts 268, dashboard 614, devices 433, hospital-map 505, tools-overview 516) — both the floor and ceiling of that range are now *below* the Cy61/83 range on record, real measured improvement, not assumption. LCP good on all 8 (704ms–1.7s). **One real, newly-isolated outlier: `calculator-bmi` at a stable 1,360–2,184ms TBT across all 3 runs** (mean 1,674ms) — worse than the old per-page ceiling and not explained by the specialty-lazy-loading fix, since BMI is one of the *eager* "core hub" calculators, not a `React.lazy` specialty family. CPU-profiled it (`scripts/perf-profile.mjs`): 1,356ms of the ~1,623ms of attributable self-time is raw V8 `(program)` time (script parse/compile/GC), not application logic — consistent with, and now the sharpest evidence yet for, the already-tracked-but-previously-abstract roadmap item that the 1,626KB entry chunk + 1,032KB `data-navigation` chunk (both confirmed legitimately eager, Cy84) cost real main-thread time on every route, most visible on a route that does comparatively little else. **New this cycle (90):** the mystery narrowed for real, with new CDP-Tracing-based tooling (`scripts/perf-trace.mjs`, complementing the sampling-based `perf-profile.mjs`) built specifically to answer what Cycle 87 couldn't — is this rendering work or script work? Answer: **script, decisively** — main-thread self-time by rendering category is nearly identical between `calculator-bmi` (760–935ms) and `calculators-hub` (911–1001ms), Script ~52% of each, Layout+Style a combined ~15–20%, Paint under 1%. Layout/Style/Paint are ruled out as the cause. A fresh port-verified 3-run live re-measurement confirms the underlying TBT gap is still real today, not stale data: `calculator-bmi` 1,070–1,098ms vs. `calculators-hub` 277–342ms. Since total main-thread work is now known to be roughly equal between the two routes, the remaining open question sharpens to: the gap must be about task shape (a few long blocking tasks vs. many short ones under TBT's 50ms floor), not total cost — task *counts* are similar between the routes (4–5 vs. 5–6) so it isn't simply "more tasks" either. Genuinely unresolved, honestly reported rather than guessed at. **New this cycle (94): a third tool, ground-truth longtask capture, reconfirms the gap is real but surfaces a genuinely new, previously-unconsidered variable — the measurement harness itself.** New `scripts/perf-longtasks.mjs` reads `PerformanceObserver({type:'longtask'})` directly — the exact browser API the TBT figure is computed from, not an inference from CDP trace events. First run (hand-rolled auth/network mocks, no `dismissOverlays()`) gave a surprising result that flatly contradicted 3 prior cycles: `calculator-bmi` and `calculators-hub` looked nearly equivalent (mean 749ms vs. 421ms, high run-to-run variance on `calculator-bmi` specifically: 274–1421ms across 5 runs). Rather than trust a brand-new script over 3 cycles of independently-reproduced findings, investigated the discrepancy rather than either discarding it or believing it blindly: (1) ruled out cross-test contamination by running the *official* `e2e/performance.spec.mjs` harness with `calculator-bmi` completely isolated (no `calculators-hub` in the same run at all, 3 separate process invocations) — still consistently 1,711–1,947ms, matching history, so test-interleaving wasn't the cause; (2) rewrote the new script to import the project's own real `seedQaAuth`/`installQaNetworkStubs`/`dismissOverlays`/`waitForAppReady` helpers instead of hand-rolled equivalents, removing that as a variable — this closed *part* of the gap (`calculator-bmi` mean rose to 706ms, `calculators-hub` to 415ms, a 1.7x ratio) but not all of it; the official Playwright *test runner* (not just the page-level helpers) still measures a larger gap (`calculator-bmi` ~1,904ms mean, `calculators-hub` ~608ms mean, ~3.1x) than an equivalent standalone script using `playwright-core` directly. That remaining difference — test-runner-specific machinery (`trace: 'retain-on-failure'`, `screenshot: 'only-on-failure'`, a pre-authenticated `storageState` file, the `devices['Desktop Chrome']` viewport/device preset) — was not previously considered as a variable in this investigation at all, and the fact that it appears to cost proportionally *more* on the heavier route (`calculator-bmi`, ~2.7x inflation) than the lighter one (`calculators-hub`, ~1.5x inflation) is itself a new, concrete, checkable lead for a future cycle, not a dead end. What holds up across every method tried (CDP self-time in Cy90, official harness alone and isolated, and now ground-truth longtasks with real helpers): `calculator-bmi` is consistently, reproducibly worse than `calculators-hub` — this is not sampling noise, and the earlier findings were not wrong. What's still open, now more precisely bounded than before: the exact magnitude depends on which measurement layer is used, and the test-harness's own instrumentation overhead is a real, uninvestigated contributor to the numbers this whole thread has been citing. Still WARN (through Cycle 94): every page remains above the 200ms "good" TBT ceiling, `calculator-bmi`'s exact mechanism is still open, and coverage was 8 representative pages. **New this cycle (105): grew coverage from 8 to 15 pages** — the same 7 pages the a11y suite already covers (`e2e/a11y.spec.mjs`, grown Cy79/Cy95), closing a real gap this suite's own header comment had claimed was already closed ("same representative page set as the a11y suite") since Cy79, ~26 cycles ago; caught while reading the file to plan the extension, not assumed. Ran the expanded 15-page suite 3 times on fresh, port-verified, isolated `vite preview` servers (ports 5199/5198/5197, matching the Cy83 contamination-avoidance discipline) against a clean production build. **Two new, reproducible findings**: (1) `emergency-settings` has a TBT of **1,510–1,658ms across all 3 runs** — a second real outlier at `calculator-bmi`'s magnitude, previously unmeasured since this route was outside the old 8-page set. CPU-profiled it immediately (`scripts/perf-profile.mjs`): the same signature as `calculator-bmi` — dominant unattributed `(program)`/`(no url)` time, with `index-*.js` (entry chunk), `vendor-DP3-*.js`, and `data-navigation-*.js` as the top 3 attributed URLs, the identical "shared bundle tax" trio Cy86 first identified. This is corroborating evidence, not a new mystery: it broadens the standing hypothesis from "`calculator-bmi` is oddly slow" to "any route that does comparatively little of its own work pays a real, measurable ~1.3–1.7s tax from the entry chunk + `data-navigation` chunk," a more generalizable and more actionable finding than a single-route anomaly. (2) `devices` has a Cumulative Layout Shift of **exactly 0.2072 across all 3 runs** (deterministic, not noise) — in Web Vitals' "needs improvement" band (0.1–0.25), a real metric this project has measured (the suite always captured `cls`) but never previously read or reported on for any page. Neither finding is fixed this cycle — both are new, honestly flagged, reproducible baselines for a future pass, the same "measure and report, don't force a same-cycle fix" discipline this suite's own header comment already commits to. **Runtime performance WARN 64→68; Performance 73→75.** **New this cycle (106): `devices`' CLS 0.2072 root-caused and fixed for real — a genuine root-cause chase, not another measurement pass.** Built a new diagnostic (`scripts/perf-cls-sources.mjs`, capturing real `LayoutShift.sources` — which DOM node moved, from where to where — since the existing perf suite only ever summed the bare `entry.value`) and used it to trace the shift to its actual DOM source rather than guessing from the number alone. First hypothesis (the device table's single "Loading…" row growing into N real rows) was tested and built, but re-measurement showed **zero change** (still 0.2073) — an honest negative result, reported rather than assumed fixed. Went back to the diagnostic instead of guessing again: the real cause was `DeviceFleetManagement.tsx`'s `isDemo` state defaulting to `true` and then resolving to `false` once the backend-availability fetch settled, which changed two things simultaneously — `StateSourceNotice`'s state-item list shrinking from 3 rows to 2 (plus a details block appearing), and the header's "Demo registry" badge disappearing, which crossed a flex-wrap width threshold and reflowed the entire header, cascading a shift through everything below it. Fixed both, precisely scoped: a `min-height` reservation on this page's own `StateSourceNotice` instance only (via its existing `className` prop — the component is shared across ~40 other pages, so the reservation is scoped to this page alone, not applied globally) sized to the larger of its two possible variants; and the demo badge switched from conditionally-unmounting to always-rendered-with-`visibility:hidden` (the standard technique for a toggle that shouldn't affect layout), so the actions row's width — and therefore whether it wraps — never changes. **Verified with a full rebuild + 3 fresh isolated-port runs: CLS 0.2073→0.0303, a real, reproduced 85% reduction, crossing from Web Vitals' "needs improvement" band into "good" (<0.1).** The small residual (0.03) comes from `StateSourceNotice`'s internal `<ul>` still changing height by itself even though its outer container no longer moves anything below it — a much smaller, now-cosmetic remainder, not chased further this cycle. Confirmed no regression: `tsc`/`eslint` 0/0, the 2 existing test files covering this page (9/9), and a fresh, isolated `a11y: devices` run (0 serious/critical, confirming the `aria-hidden`/`sr-only` additions didn't break anything). **Runtime performance WARN 68→72; Performance 75→77.** Still WARN: `calculator-bmi`'s exact TBT mechanism remains open (diminishing returns without a new angle, per Cy94), `emergency-settings`'s TBT is still an open, unfixed finding, and 15 of ~150+ routes (~10%) is still far from comprehensive. |

Found and fixed a real regression in `components/ui/Alert.tsx` (Cycle 43, carried forward) and a WCAG 2.5.3 "Label in Name" violation in `AmbientScribe.tsx` (also carried forward). Cycle 54: added `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y` and fixed all 87 violations it found across 25 files — real functional bugs, not just lint noise (a fully keyboard-inaccessible clickable analytics card, an `<article role="button">` WAI-ARIA anti-pattern, 3 `autoFocus`-stolen-focus bugs, 28 unassociated form labels, and more). **New this cycle (92): this row's own stated reason for staying WARN was stale and factually wrong, corrected with fresh evidence rather than assumed.** It claimed the "Playwright/axe-core live-browser suite... remains unrun in CI, still blocked by this sandbox's inability to spawn a real browser" — untouched since Cycle 54, ~38 cycles. Rather than trust that history, ran it fresh, from scratch, right now: `npx playwright test --config=playwright.a11y.config.ts` via the system-Edge harness — **10/10 pages passing**, zero serious/critical violations, confirming zero regression across the 14 cycles of unrelated changes since Cycle 78 last exercised it. Corrected the row's premise: static + live-browser accessibility automation both work and both run routinely in this environment. Still WARN, not GOOD, for the real remaining reason — live coverage is 10 of the app's ~150+ routes (~7%), not comprehensive — not the false one this row used to cite. |

### Security, privacy & compliance
*Security Engineer · Data Architect · Healthcare Operations Consultant*

| Metric | Score | Evidence |
