| Perimeter security | GOOD 60 | Unchanged. |
| Authorization depth (RBAC) | WARN 73 (was 73, unchanged this cycle) | **New this cycle (102): the entire remaining RBAC triage list closed — every controller checked out clean, closing a thread open since Cycle 98.** With Cycle 101's corrected risk model in hand (a global `TenantIsolationGuard` floor plus per-route service-layer checks are both legitimate, real authorization mechanisms this project uses alongside route decorators), read every one of the ~17 remaining controllers in full rather than sampling a few and generalizing. They fall into four groups, each safe for a different, verified reason: **(1) self-scoped to the caller** — `notification` (12/12 routes reference `req.user`), `user-activity` (4/4), `personalization` (5/5), `two-factor` (5/5), `user-profile` (11 refs/8 routes), `memory` (every query filters by `userId` from the JWT, confirmed in Cycle 98's own prep reading) — no cross-user data is reachable because the caller's own id is the only key ever used, never client-suppliable. `biometric.controller.ts`'s one apparent gap (`POST verify` has no `req.user`) is correct by necessity: it's the pre-authentication login endpoint itself, the same reason a password-login route can't require a JWT. **(2) service-layer `assertAdmin`/`assertMember` checks, not controller decorators** — `product-catalog.controller.ts` (27 routes; every org-scoped route calls `organizationsService.assertMemberForUser()` or `assertAdminForUser()` before proceeding, confirmed by reading each one, including `updateConfiguration`, whose check lives inside `OrganizationsService.updateConfiguration()` itself rather than the controller — the same call, just one layer down); `settings-features.controller.ts` (properly `@TenantScoped({admin: 'organization', permissions: [CONFIGURE_SYSTEM]})` on its one mutating route, with full audit logging — this one was actually mis-listed as "bare `AuthGuard('jwt')` only" back in Cycle 99, another small correction). **(3) global-floor-plus-no-real-cross-user-data** — `evaluation`, `cost-optimizer`, `training` (AI/ML engineering dashboards with zero `organizationId`/`patientId`/`userId` references anywhere in their services — genuinely global, platform-level tooling, not tenant or patient data); `tool-orchestrator`, `tool-calling`, `native-ai` (clinical-calculator/AI-tool execution over caller-supplied request-body parameters, not stored-record lookups by id — the same stateless-computation shape already confirmed safe for `emergency-os.research.controller.ts` in Cycle 101, each gated by a real `assertEntitlementLaunchFromRequest()` check besides); `simulation` (explicitly self-labeled `sourceStatus: 'demo-local-state'` in its own entity shape — a shared, non-persistent training sandbox with no `userId` field on its data at all, confirmed by reading the service, not assumed from the name). **(4) token-as-authorization** — `workspace-invitations.controller.ts`'s `preview`/`accept` routes use the invitation token itself as the credential, the standard invite-link pattern used by every SaaS product with shareable invites — correctly unauthenticated for preview, JWT-gated for accept. `tenant-context.controller.ts` is fully self-scoped (`getContext`) or `@TenantScoped({admin: 'organization'})`-gated (`isolationAudit`). **No code changed this cycle — all 17 were already correct.** **No score movement** — consistent with this project's standing rule that verification work doesn't earn the credit a shipped fix does, even when (as here) it's thorough enough to fully retire a roadmap item. **The P1 roadmap item is now closed** (see below) — not because every controller in the backend has a `@RequirePermission` decorator (most legitimately don't need one), but because every one that was flagged as a potential gap has now actually been read, not just counted. **Lesson closing out this five-cycle thread**: the original Cycle 98 census asked one narrow question ("does this route have a `@RequirePermission` decorator") of a codebase that answers "is this safe" in at least four different legitimate ways — decorators, a global tenant guard, manual service-layer asserts, and structural safety from operating only on caller-supplied, non-identity-bearing data. A scan that only checks for one of the four will always overcount the real gap; the fix, demonstrated four separate times now (Cy99, Cy100, Cy101, Cy102), was never a smarter grep — it was reading the code. **Cycle 101: the RBAC triage's risk model itself was incomplete — a third refinement in this thread, this time not a wrong number but a missing piece of the architecture.** Went to triage the next controller on the list (`automation-audit.controller.ts`) and noticed `listEvents()` does `req.tenantContext?.organizationId \|\| tenantId` — a client-suppliable query-param fallback that looked, on first read, like a real cross-tenant bypass if `tenantContext` were ever unpopulated. Checking whether that's actually reachable led to a bigger discovery: `TenantIsolationGuard` is registered as a **global `APP_GUARD`** (`tenant-context.module.ts`, confirmed wired into `AppModule`), meaning it runs on *every* route in the entire application regardless of what an individual controller's own `@UseGuards()` lists — a mechanism none of Cycles 98-100's analysis accounted for, since all three only looked at guards a controller declared locally. Read the guard's own logic again with this in mind: unless a route carries `@SkipTenantIsolation()`, the guard unconditionally throws 403 before the handler ever runs if the caller's resolved `tenantContext` lacks a valid `organizationId`/`workspaceId` — meaning `automation-audit.controller.ts`'s `\|\| tenantId` fallback is dead code in practice (the guard would have already rejected the request before reaching it if `tenantContext.organizationId` were missing), not a live bypass. **This means every one of the "~19 controllers with no authorization-capable guard" from Cycles 98-100 was mischaracterized as "reachable by any authenticated user of any role" when the accurate description is "reachable by any authenticated, legitimate member of *some* organization+workspace, any role"** — a real, global floor this thread never credited, though it still doesn't enforce role-based permission beyond that. Checked exactly which controllers actually skip this floor (only 4 in the whole backend, via `grep -rl "@SkipTenantIsolation"`): `collaboration-hub.controller.ts` (fixed Cycle 100), `organizations.controller.ts`, `workspaces.controller.ts`, `emergency-os.research.controller.ts`. Read the latter 3 in full rather than assume risk from the label alone — all three check out clean. `organizations.controller.ts` and `workspaces.controller.ts` both skip the floor only on routes with no tenant-boundary path param (list-for-me, create, switch-active-workspace — nothing to compare a path param against yet), and every route that *does* carry an `:organizationId`/`:workspaceId` uses `@OrganizationScoped()`/`@WorkspaceScoped()` (with `admin:`/`permissions:` requirements on the mutating ones), which sets the same `TENANT_SCOPE_KEY` metadata the guard reads — properly, individually tenant-scoped, not a gap. `emergency-os.research.controller.ts` carries its own header comment documenting a real prior fix (these research/demo endpoints — federated EMS triage, LMECS client selection, AI call interrogation, digital-twin simulation — "previously had no guards at all... accepted patient data from completely unauthenticated requests," now require `AuthGuard('jwt')`) — and since every one of its routes operates on caller-supplied request-body payloads rather than looking up a stored patient record by id, there's no cross-user data to leak even without a tenant-scope check; the meaningful floor (must be authenticated) is already the whole story. **No code changed this cycle** — every controller actually investigated turned out already correct, either by a real global mechanism this thread had never accounted for, or by prior, uncredited fixes. **No score movement**, consistent with this project's standing rule that verification work — confirming something is already fine — doesn't move a gauge the way shipping a fix does; this row holds at 73 pending the next controller that's actually found to need a change. **Practical effect on the remaining triage list**: down to ~17 controllers (`emergency-os.research` and `workspaces` now confirmed clean, off the list), and the real remaining question for each is narrower and lower-urgency than Cycles 98-100 implied — not "is this reachable by a stranger" (no, the global floor already blocks that for all of them), but "should this also require a specific role/permission beyond just being a legitimate hospital-org member," which is a meaningfully smaller class of risk. **Lesson: this is the third time this exact investigation thread has had to correct its own risk model in three consecutive cycles (Cy99: the census tool was wrong; Cy100: modeled correctly but scope-tested before generalizing; Cy101: the whole "what counts as unprotected" definition was incomplete) — each correction was worth doing rather than plowing ahead, and the pattern holds: before fixing a controller because a scan says it's "unprotected," find out what "protected" actually means in this specific app's architecture, including guards that aren't declared where you're looking.** **Cycle 100: the one real gap Cycle 99's correction confirmed — `collaboration-hub.controller.ts` — investigated carefully rather than fixed on the strength of its "0/23" label alone, since that label came from the exact census methodology Cycle 99 just found unreliable.** Read the module's ~600-line service in full before touching anything: it turns out to be a genuinely well-built, defense-in-depth system with its own documented history of two real, already-fixed cross-tenant bugs (a department-channel auto-join that used to let any org join any other org's channel by guessing its id; a message-reaction/pin/attachment path that used to let any org touch a message that wasn't in its own channel) — both fixed by routing every lookup through an org-scoped `getChannel()` plus a channel-membership check in `assertAccess()`, the same "Slack/Teams scope by membership" model the controller's own header comment documents. 21 of its 23 routes are legitimately safe this way despite carrying no `@RequirePermission` — membership itself is the real gate, and a decorator would be redundant. **One route breaks the pattern**: `GET patients/:patientId/thread` is the entry point that *grants* membership to a patient's clinical thread in the first place, for any `patientId` the caller supplies — any authenticated user in the org could call it and be auto-joined into (and thereafter read/post in) any patient's clinical discussion thread, with zero PHI-specific permission check before this fix, unlike the org-scoped-then-membership-gated pattern every other mutation in the file follows. Fixed: added `AuthorizationGuard` to the controller's class-level guard chain (a no-op for the other 22 routes, which carry no decorator) and `@RequirePermission(Permission.READ_PHI)` to this one route. **Also found and closed a separate, honest gap while here**: this entire module — the controller plus its service — had zero test files anywhere in the repo, confirmed via a repo-wide grep for the class names, not assumed from a missing filename. Added `collaboration-hub.controller.spec.ts` (the first test this module has ever had): 3 tests locking in the class now requires `AuthorizationGuard`, `getPatientThread` requires `READ_PHI`, and the other 22 routes explicitly stay undecorated (a regression guard against someone "fixing" them into permission-gates that would break the membership model). Verified beyond the unit tests: a fresh live boot against an empty SQLite database (matching Cycle 96's boot-verification discipline, since this changes guard wiring — exactly the class of change that can pass isolated module tests but fail on a full app compile) came up clean, `/health/live` 200, the collaboration route correctly 401s with no token, no DI resolution error for the newly-added `AuthorizationGuard` (its `AuditService` dependency was already available via `CollaborationHubModule`'s existing `AuditModule` import). Full backend regression **245/245 suites, 1,917/1,917 tests** (was 244/1,914 — the +1 suite/+3 tests are the new spec file, zero other breakage); `tsc`/eslint 0/0. **Authorization depth (RBAC) WARN 70→73; Security & privacy 93→94.** Left alone, correctly: the other ~19 controllers on Cycle 99's corrected triage list (`memory`, `compliance`, `automation-audit`, `cost-optimizer`, `tool-orchestrator`, `evaluation`, and the rest) still need the same per-controller read-the-actual-code treatment this row just modeled — several are plausibly fine by design (self-scoped personal data, matching this same module's own membership-scoping philosophy), a few plausibly aren't, and finding out which is which is exactly the item this cycle didn't try to rush. **Cycle 99: Cycle 98's own project-wide census (below) was itself wrong, caught and corrected the very next cycle.** That census grepped only for `@RequirePermission`, missing that `@Permissions(...)`/`@AnyPermission(...)` set the identical `PERMISSIONS_KEY` metadata `AuthorizationGuard` actually reads, and separately mis-scored any controller using a class-level decorator as covering only 1 route instead of all of them. Re-checked every named example against source directly, not re-grepped: `governance.controller.ts` is **77/77** gated (not 0/77), `sentinel.controller.ts` **17/17** (not 0/17), `patient-clinical-data.controller.ts` **12/12** (not 0/12), `rag.controller.ts` **2/2** via one class-level decorator (not 0/2) — 4 of 5 named examples were wrong; `collaboration-hub.controller.ts` (0/23, no authorization-capable guard in its chain at all) was the one that held up. See the corrected P1 roadmap item for the full accurate picture and the now-precisely-scoped ~20-controller triage list. **No score change either direction**: the concrete Cycle 98 fix (2 `emergency-os.controller.ts` routes, tested) was, and remains, the only thing this row was ever credited for — the flawed census carried zero score weight in Cycle 98 and its correction carries none now, consistent with this project's standing rule that findings (right or wrong) don't move gauges on their own. **Lesson: the "stale WARN blocker" self-check this project runs on old rows applies just as hard to a claim made one cycle ago — re-verify against source, not against your own last grep, especially when the number is dramatic (0/77 should have been suspicious on its own).** **Cycle 98, superseded by the above but kept for the record: a live recurrence of a previously-fixed bug class, found and closed with tests.** `POST patients/:patientId/document-artifacts/extract` and `PATCH .../document-artifacts/:artifactId/review` in `emergency-os.controller.ts` — routes that write AI-extracted clinical findings into a patient's record — had no `@RequirePermission` at all (any authenticated user, any role, confirmed via `AuthorizationGuard`'s own "no decorator → allow" source) and skipped the `EmergencyPatientAuditService.logPatientAccess()` HIPAA audit call their own sibling GET route performs on the same resource. Identical bug shape to one already fixed 2026-07-10 (`07e5df82`) in `ArtifactsController.create`/`update`, whose own spec file's test pattern (`Reflect.getMetadata(PERMISSIONS_KEY, handler)`) was reused directly here. Fixed: `@RequirePermission(Permission.WRITE_PHI)` added to both, matching the controller's own `ems/handoff`/`reception/handoff` convention, plus the missing audit call wired in. 4 new tests (2 metadata checks + 2 audit-call behavioral checks), `emergency-os` module **13 suites / 70 tests**, full backend **244/244 suites, 1,914/1,914 tests**, `tsc`/eslint 0/0. **A project-wide `@RequirePermission` census run while scoping this found the pattern is much larger than these 2 routes** — see the Cycle 98 headline and the new P1 roadmap item for exact per-controller counts (`governance.controller.ts` 0/77, `collaboration-hub.controller.ts` 0/23, `patient-clinical-data.controller.ts` 0/12, `sentinel.controller.ts` 0/17, `rag.controller.ts` 0/2, `emergency-os.controller.ts` itself only 5/72 even post-fix) — confirmed not hidden by any class-level decorator, and confirmed `TenantIsolationGuard`-protected controllers (`ai.controller.ts`, `platform-assets.controller.ts`) have a real cross-org isolation floor even without one, a meaningfully smaller gap than the `AuthorizationGuard`-only controllers with no floor at all. This is a different, deeper dimension than what the earlier "completed 3-cycle audit" (below) actually checked — doesn't contradict it, just was never in its scope. Not attempted as a drive-by fix — closing it needs a per-route permission decision across dozens of controllers, scoped as its own dedicated-pass roadmap item rather than risked as a fast sweep. **Prior audit, carried forward:** no open questions remain from the completed 3-cycle frontend/backend permission-*catalog*-mirroring audit — the backend `sentinel` module's 7 permissions were entirely absent from the frontend's `backendPermissionCatalog.ts` mirror (`security.contract.test.ts` had already flagged this via `missing frontend mirror for VIEW_SENTINEL_COMMAND`), and 4 real, nav-visible routes (`/triage`, `/hospital-map`, `/predictive-analytics`, `/executive`) were missing from `admin`/`ed_manager`/`charge_nurse`/`triage_nurse`'s own permission-model route lists in `emergencyRolePermissions.ts` — a genuine "sidebar shows a link the role's own access-check would reject" latent bug, traced to the Stage-2 nav-ceiling fix never being propagated to this separate permission model. Both closed. |

| Deployment & observability | WARN 64 (was 48) | **First real audit of this row ever done — it had sat at WARN 48 with zero attached evidence since baseline.** Found substantial, real, production-grade observability infrastructure that was never credited anywhere: a comprehensive multi-component health check (`backend/src/api/health.routes.ts`) covering MongoDB, SQL/TypeORM, 27 registered internal services, WebSocket servers, an MQTT broker, and 2 external APIs (MoH FHIR, wearables) — each check individually timeout-guarded (`withTimeout()`, default 2s), correctly distinguishing `critical` from non-critical components so one optional dependency being down degrades rather than fails the whole response, plus a separate fast `/health/live` liveness route with no dependency fan-out for boot polling. A full Prometheus metrics system (`backend/src/modules/metrics/`) using real `prom-client` Counters/Histograms/Gauges across HTTP (with a path-normalizing interceptor that collapses UUIDs/numeric IDs to `:id` so per-request cardinality can't explode the metric set), database queries, cache operations, errors, clinical-tool invocations, RAG retrieval, emergency detection, active users, and OpenAI cost tracking — including a real, already-documented security decision in the source (a `user_id` label was deliberately removed from cost metrics since `/metrics` has no auth guard by Prometheus-scrape convention, and per-user cost-by-ID on an unauthenticated endpoint would have been a real PII leak). Both are genuinely wired in, not dead code: `MetricsModule` is imported and `HttpMetricsInterceptor` is registered as a global `APP_INTERCEPTOR` in `app.module.ts`, confirmed by reading the module, not assumed. **Verified live, not just from source**: booted the backend fresh and curled every endpoint. `/health/live` and `/health` returned real, structured, honest data (a genuinely `degraded` overall status when MongoDB wasn't configured and 2 external APIs weren't reachable — exactly the honest-degradation behavior this project's own methodology values). **`/metrics` itself was broken** — the controller's own doc comment says `GET /metrics`, "Compatible with Prometheus scraper" (the standard scrape convention expects an unprefixed `/metrics`), but `main.ts`'s `app.setGlobalPrefix('api', { exclude: ['health', ''] })` never excluded `'metrics'`, so the live route was actually `/api/metrics` — a 404 for any standard `scrape_config` pointed at the documented path. Confirmed this wasn't a new assumption: `backend/src/app.module.spec.ts` already asserted `/metrics` and `/metrics/(.*)` belong in `STATIC_ASSET_EXCLUDES` (the SPA-fallback protection list), meaning the codebase's own test suite already expected `/metrics` to be a real, unprefixed, production route — just nothing had wired `setGlobalPrefix` to match. Fixed by adding `'metrics'` to the exclude list. **Verified live after the fix**: fresh boot, `curl /metrics` → `200` with 61 real Prometheus metric families (`process_cpu_user_seconds_total`, `nodejs_eventloop_lag_seconds`, and the full custom set above); `curl /api/metrics` → `404`, confirming a clean move rather than an accidental duplicate route. Full backend regression **244/244 suites, 1,910/1,910 tests**; `app.module.spec.ts` and the metrics module's own suite individually re-confirmed; `tsc`/eslint 0/0. Still WARN, not GOOD: no real Prometheus server/Grafana dashboard has been stood up against this endpoint in this sandbox (infra-gated, same class of limitation as the Postgres adversarial-pack item under Security), MongoDB health was exercised only in its honest not-configured path, graceful-shutdown/SIGTERM handling hasn't been audited, and CI/CD coherence (the sibling row) remains untouched this cycle. |
| Accessibility (live-browser WCAG) | WARN 66 (was 58) | **New this cycle (95): real coverage growth, not bookkeeping — 10→15 routes, and the expansion found a real critical-impact violation.** Added 5 pages spanning archetypes the suite didn't cover yet: a patient board (`/emergency/whiteboard`), a chart-heavy dashboard (`/emergency/analytics`), a settings form (`/emergency/settings`), a queue/list view (`/triage`), and an interactive scenario list (`/simulation`). The very first run caught a real defect: `EmergencySettings.tsx`'s per-alert-rule severity `<select>` (Info/Warning/Critical) had **zero accessible name** — no wrapping `<label>`, no `aria-label`, nothing — axe flagged it `critical` impact, correctly, since a screen-reader user landing on that control would have no way to know what it controls. A sibling helper (`SettingsField`) in the same file already wraps its own selects in a real `<label>`; this one raw `<select>` bypassed that pattern. Fixed with `aria-label={`${rule} severity`}`, matching the rule name already used as the adjacent checkbox's own label. **15/15 passing, zero serious/critical, verified 3 ways**: isolated single-route run, full-suite serial (1 worker), and full-suite default (2 workers) — one apparent failure on `ems-pipeline` during the very first default-config run turned out to be 2-worker resource contention, not a real defect (confirmed by both an isolated re-run and a serial full-suite re-run, both clean; consistent with this project's own previously-documented parallel-worker flake pattern, e.g. `pilotWalkthrough`). `tsc`/eslint clean; `EmergencySettings.test.tsx` 4/4 unaffected. First real axe-core scan of this project ever executed (Cycle 59; prior a11y work was static `jsx-a11y` lint only). Ran `e2e/a11y.spec.mjs` (WCAG 2.1 A/AA) via the system-Edge workaround, found a systemic contrast-token bug (`--medical-ink-muted`/`--medical-ink-subtle` both failing 4.5:1 against white) responsible for the large majority of violations across every page, fixed it at the token root, and re-scanned to confirm a real, measured 259→~48 violation-node drop (81%). Coverage grew 8→10 routes by Cycle 79 (both new routes — Reception and EMS interactive-AI workspaces — passing zero-serious/critical on first scan) and the top-level gauge was credited then; **this row's own number/text were never updated to match and had drifted stale** (still said "8... routes" as of Cycle 91, 33 cycles later) — corrected here as a bookkeeping fix, not a new score claim, since the underlying work was already credited in Cycle 79's gauge move. **Re-verified fresh this cycle (92), not cited from memory**: ran the full 10-page suite from scratch via `npx playwright test --config=playwright.a11y.config.ts` — **10/10 passing, zero serious/critical**, confirming no regression across the 14 cycles of unrelated changes since this suite was last exercised (Cycle 78). Still WARN, not GOOD: only 10 of the app's ~150+ routes are covered (~7%), several smaller component-specific contrast issues remain identified-but-open (headline item 31, not re-checked this cycle), and Lighthouse performance profiling through this same browser path still hasn't been attempted. |

## What's genuinely solid (cumulative)

- Frontend + backend `tsc --noEmit`: 0 errors, now at the **full, honest scope** including test files (previously hidden 1,341 errors, now closed).
- Frontend + backend `eslint`: 0 errors across both trees.
- The repo's own inline-style CI gate: **passing** (475/690) for the first time since Cycle 0.
- Zero remaining bare-boolean ARIA attribute violations across the frontend.
- 208/208 backend test suites, 1688/1688 tests passing (up from 192/1487).
- **The frontend/backend clinical-tool contract is now fully reconciled**: every one of the 37 real backend POST executors is correctly mirrored on the frontend (`REGISTRY_ID_TO_ORCHESTRATOR_TOOL`), and the 37-file / 165-test suite drift that stale mapping had caused is fully fixed and re-verified (825/857 frontend files passing, 208/208 backend suites) — real capability that was previously unreachable from the product side (`orchestratorTool` always resolved `null` for 15+ working backend calculators) is now actually wired end to end.
- **37 clinical tools have real, verified backend execution logic (up from 3 at Cycle 0)** — the entire originally-scoped batch-executor roadmap item is now closed, spot-checked against 17+ known clinical scenarios via real HTTP boots across 4 batches.
- Found and removed a real duplicate (`corrected-sodium`/`osmolal-gap` dead code in `endocrineMetabolicCalculators.ts`).
- Real AES-256-GCM PHI/PII encryption at rest for both entities that collect it.
- A completed RBAC audit with zero open authorization questions.
- `npm audit` restored to working order on the frontend (was silently broken); both frontend (1) and backend (4) finding counts unchanged and understood.
- Main entry bundle chunk: 1,306.56 KB → 727.81 KB across two prior passes (44% cut), holding steady this pass.
- A real, previously-invisible entry-point bug (`main.jsx` → `main.tsx`) fixed.
- Reception module: 5 audited, evidence-traced UX/accessibility findings fixed (dead escalation UI now live, inaccessible badge-overflow control fixed, a reusable stage-timeline pattern shipped, unified AI-surface identity, dead prop removed).
- A real cross-role accent-color token system shipped and correctly scoped (verified via grep: 348 references to the old shared accent color across 114 files, 0 raw hardcodes bypassing the token layer — the new system cascades without touching those files).
- **2 real, pre-existing architecture bugs found by actually rendering the app in a browser** (not just static analysis) — a CSS container-query width collapse, and an accent-token scope leak into a cross-role safety-UI invariant — both fixed and verified by direct DOM measurement.
- 3 new test files / 14 new tests closing a real coverage gap on today's new components.
- **16 of the 32 pre-existing frontend test failures root-caused and fixed this cycle**, each traced to a specific cause (a flaky test-infra timeout, real product drift after already-shipped fixes, or a genuine RBAC/route-contract gap) — none loosened or deleted to make them pass.
- The Sentinel module (fleet/CAD-ingest command center — 7 permissions, 17 HTTP routes) is now fully mirrored across all 3 frontend contract catalogs it was missing from.
- Frontend `npm audit`: 0 vulnerabilities (was 1).
- A user-reported header UI duplication (profile switcher shown twice) traced through 4 flip-flopped commits and consolidated to the codebase's own documented single-control design; a third, fully orphaned demo-switching implementation removed with zero dangling references.
- **The app's entire sky-blue accent-color identity, restored** after confirming a real regression (not a redesign) had silently flattened it to monochrome — the most significant single bug found across this whole 43-cycle exercise, since it affected the visible color of buttons, links, focus rings, and CTAs throughout the product.
- **61 more instances of a silent CSS-syntax-dropping bug fixed across 25 files** (same bug class as one fixed once before in an earlier cycle, now confirmed far more widespread) — every fix verified via a brace-balance check, not just visual inspection.
- 6 of the 7-file CSS-structural-contract cluster fully fixed; a git stash mishap during the investigation was diagnosed and recovered from with zero data loss, verified file-by-file.
- **The entire pre-existing frontend test-failure backlog is now closed bar one narrow, already-scoped gap** — the final 7 flagged files all root-caused individually this cycle, 2 of them real product bugs (an `Alert.tsx` ARIA-role regression, a chat-suggestion ranking bug that silently hid billing/audit/notification suggestions behind 37 clinical-tool executors) fixed at the source, not just in the test.
- Full frontend suite: 832/836 files, 11547/11556 tests passing; the remaining 4 "failures" confirmed pure Vitest worker-teardown noise (pass 10/10 in isolation), not a real defect.
- **All 39 catalogued clinical tools that were ever scoped as "portable/derivable" now have real backend executors** — the last 2 (Wells DVT, ABG interpreter) closed the entire originally-scoped roadmap item, each independently verified against standard clinical references with 34 hand-derived tests passing on the first run.
- A real, live dark-mode feature (persistence, system-preference detection, a working header toggle) reconciled against 4 stale tests and a second wave of the accent-color regression (54 more instances across 16 files) found and fixed along the way.
- **Corrected two significantly stale category scores** (AI Chief orchestration depth, EMS & triage workflows) that had sat frozen for many cycles on "not touched this pass" — both had substantial real, tested infrastructure (23 files / 94 tests) the frozen scores no longer reflected.
- 17 real ripple-effect test failures from registering 2 new executors, plus one genuine pre-existing bug (`ThemeToggle` crashing 2 header-rendering test files that predated the dark-mode commit's provider requirement) — all fixed and individually re-verified; full frontend suite re-run twice for stability at 836/837 and 837/837 files.
- **The entire missing-schema P0 closed**: all 65 entity-declared tables now have a real migration (was 19 of 65) — 46 tables, including `users`, `organizations`/`workspaces`, `subscriptions`, the platform-assets and product-catalog suites, `audit_logs`, and 9 Sentinel tables, that previously existed only under `synchronize: true` and would never have been created on a real Postgres deployment. Verified with a full forward→revert-to-empty→forward round trip (27/27 migrations each direction, 0 errors), not just a one-way run.
- **Real static accessibility linting added for the first time** (`eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y`) and every one of the 87 violations it found fixed with a targeted repair — including a fully keyboard-inaccessible clickable dashboard card, a WAI-ARIA role-override anti-pattern, and a real "focus keeps getting stolen back on every keystroke" bug caught and avoided before it could ship (an inline `ref` callback for autofocus that would have refired on every re-render).

Four legitimate safety patterns account for all of them, each confirmed by reading the actual code rather than assumed: self-scoped-to-caller (`notification`, `user-activity`, `personalization`, `two-factor`, `user-profile`, `memory`, `biometric` — the one apparent gap in `biometric` is its pre-auth login-verify route, correctly unguarded); service-layer `assertAdmin`/`assertMember` checks instead of controller decorators (`product-catalog`'s 27 routes, `settings-features`'s properly `@TenantScoped({admin:'organization', permissions:[CONFIGURE_SYSTEM]})`-gated mutation — this one was actually mis-listed as ungated back in Cycle 99, a small further correction); global-floor-plus-no-real-cross-user-data (`evaluation`/`cost-optimizer`/`training` — zero tenant/patient/user references anywhere in their services, genuinely global platform tooling; `tool-orchestrator`/`tool-calling`/`native-ai` — stateless computation over caller-supplied payloads, not stored-record lookups, each with a real entitlement check; `simulation` — explicitly self-labeled demo/non-persistent shared state with no `userId` concept at all); token-as-authorization (`workspace-invitations`'s invite-link pattern, `tenant-context`'s self-scoped/admin-gated routes). See the Authorization depth (RBAC) row above for the full per-controller writeup. No score moved (verification-only). This closes the roadmap thread that ran Cycles 98-102: the original census asked one narrow question of a codebase that answers "is this safe" four different legitimate ways — the fix was never a smarter grep, it was reading the code, four separate times over. | — |
| P1-SUPERSEDED | ~~Individually triage the remaining ~17 controllers with no role-based permission check (all already sit behind a global org-membership floor)~~ | **Re-scoped 2026-07-18 (Cycle 101) — the risk model itself was incomplete, corrected before triaging the next controller.** `TenantIsolationGuard` turns out to be a global `APP_GUARD` (`TenantContextModule`, confirmed imported into `AppModule`) that runs on every route in the app regardless of a controller's own `@UseGuards()` list, and unconditionally requires a valid `organizationId`/`workspaceId` tenant context or 403s — unless the specific route carries `@SkipTenantIsolation()`. Only 4 controllers in the entire backend do: `collaboration-hub` (closed Cycle 100), `organizations.controller.ts`, `workspaces.controller.ts`, `emergency-os.research.controller.ts`. Read the latter 3 in full this cycle rather than assumed from the label: all three check out clean — `organizations`/`workspaces` skip the floor only on routes with no tenant-boundary path param (self-scoped to the caller, or creating a tenant that doesn't exist yet), and every route that does carry `:organizationId`/`:workspaceId` uses `@OrganizationScoped()`/`@WorkspaceScoped()` (with `admin:`/`permissions:` on the mutating ones) instead; `emergency-os.research.controller.ts` carries its own comment documenting a real prior fix (used to accept patient data fully unauthenticated, now requires JWT) and operates only on caller-supplied request-body payloads, not stored records, so there's no cross-user data to leak even without tenant scoping. **No code changed — every controller actually investigated this cycle was already correct.** List shrinks from ~19 to ~17 (`emergency-os.research`, `workspaces` now confirmed clean). **The remaining ~17 were also mischaracterized**: every one of them (none skip the global guard) is already gated to "any legitimate member of some org+workspace," not "any authenticated user, full stop" — the real open question per controller is narrower now: does it also need a specific *role* check on top of that floor, not whether it's reachable by a stranger. No score change (verification-only, nothing shipped). **1 of the original 21 closed 2026-07-18 (Cycle 100): `collaboration-hub.controller.ts`.** Read its ~600-line service in full rather than trusting the "0/23" label: 21 of 23 routes are legitimately safe via a real, already-battle-tested channel-membership authorization model (`assertAccess()`), with its own documented history of two prior real cross-tenant fixes. The one real gap — `GET patients/:patientId/thread`, which *grants* thread membership for any `patientId` with no PHI check — is fixed (`AuthorizationGuard` + `@RequirePermission(READ_PHI)`), tested (first-ever spec file for this module, 3 tests), and live-boot-verified (fresh SQLite boot, no DI crash from the new guard). Full backend regression 245/245 suites, 1,917/1,917 tests. See the Authorization depth (RBAC) row above for the full writeup. **Remaining ~17** (`memory`, `product-catalog`, `notification`, `compliance`, `evaluation`, `tool-calling`, `user-activity`, `personalization`, `two-factor`, `training`, `biometric`, `simulation`, `user-profile`, `tool-orchestrator`, `native-ai`, `workspace-invitations`, `tenant-context`, `cost-optimizer`, `automation-audit`, `settings-features` — `emergency-os.research` and `workspaces` closed off this list in Cycle 101, `automation-audit` re-checked in Cycle 101 too and confirmed fine, see the Authorization depth row above) still need the same read-the-actual-service-before-judging treatment `collaboration-hub` just got — worth doing one controller at a time, not as a batch, since this cycle's own finding (a module that looked like a blank "0/23" gap was actually 21/23 fine and 1/23 real) shows why. **Prior correction, 2026-07-18 (Cycle 99) — Cycle 98's own census was itself wrong, caught and fixed one cycle later.** That census grepped only for `@RequirePermission`, missing the `@Permissions(...)`/`@AnyPermission(...)` decorator forms entirely (all three set the identical `PERMISSIONS_KEY` metadata `AuthorizationGuard` reads), and separately mis-scored any controller using a **class-level** decorator as if it only covered one route instead of all of them. Re-verified every named example directly against source, not re-grepped: `platform-systems/governance.controller.ts` is **77/77** gated (not 0/77 as Cy98 claimed), `sentinel.controller.ts` **17/17** (not 0/17), `platform-systems/patient-clinical-data.controller.ts` **12/12** (not 0/12), `rag.controller.ts` **2/2** via one class-level `@Permissions(VIEW_ANALYTICS)` (not 0/2) — 4 of the 5 named examples were wrong. `telemetry.controller.ts`, `fleet.controller.ts`, `hospital-map.controller.ts`, `surveillance.controller.ts`, `clinical-alerts.controller.ts` are likewise fully gated via the same class-level pattern, never checked in Cy98. `emergency-os.controller.ts` 5/72 was accurate (verified via a full source read both cycles, not a grep). The one named example that held up: `collaboration-hub.controller.ts`, genuinely 0/23 with no `AuthorizationGuard`/`TenantIsolationGuard` in its guard chain at all (only bare `AuthGuard('jwt')`) — real, confirmed. **The true remaining pattern, now precisely scoped**: ~20 controllers (`collaboration-hub`, `memory`, `product-catalog`, `notification`, `compliance`, `evaluation`, `tool-calling`, `user-activity`, `personalization`, `two-factor`, `training`, `biometric`, `simulation`, `emergency-os.research`, `user-profile`, `tool-orchestrator`, `native-ai`, `workspaces`, `workspace-invitations`, `tenant-context`, `cost-optimizer`, `automation-audit`, `settings-features`) use bare `AuthGuard('jwt')` with no authorization-capable guard wired in at all — architecturally different from "guard present, decorator forgotten." Spot-checking the pattern this project actually uses elsewhere (`users.controller.ts`'s ungated `GET profile` next to its `WRITE_PHI`-gated `PATCH`; `audit.controller.ts`'s ungated self-scoped `GET my-logs` next to its gated cross-user log views) suggests several of these 20 are plausibly fine by design (self-scoped personal data: profile, personalization, two-factor, tenant-context) — but that can't be assumed without checking each one individually, exactly the judgment call this item already called for, just against accurate numbers now. No score moved for this correction — the concrete Cycle 98 fix (2 `emergency-os.controller.ts` routes, tested) is unaffected and was never credited on the census, only on the fix itself. | High |

| P2 | Fix the Total Blocking Time / page-weight finding from the Cycle 60 performance measurement | **Cycle 103 sync note: this row itself had drifted stale** — its own text still read "a trace-based (not sampling-based) profile is the remaining path to a real root cause" as an open next step, but that step was already taken twice (Cycles 90 and 94, both already documented in the Runtime performance row under Frontend & design system above) and this table row was simply never updated to match. No new investigation run this cycle, no score claimed for this correction — the substance was already accurately recorded elsewhere, only this row's copy lagged. Per the standing "calculator-bmi has diminishing returns, don't re-attempt without a new angle" note, deliberately not re-opening the mystery itself this cycle. **Substantially closed 2026-07-15 (Cycle 61)** — root-caused via a real CDP CPU profile (which also exposed that Cycle 60's numbers came from a leftover dev server; corrected). Two fixes shipped and measured: `routePatternMatches` regex-cache (mean TBT −14%, A/B-verified) and the `manualChunks` fusion fix that stopped every route from executing the 1,226KB calculator catalog + 11 specialty chunks at startup (preloads ~20→8 files, LCP improved 7/8 pages). Sub-items (a)-(d) now **all closed or superseded**: (a) per-specialty-family lazy-loading — confirmed already shipped, Cycle 85; (b) `analytics`/`vendor-charts` fusion closed Cycle 83; (d) **Cycle 86 took the fresh TBT re-measurement this item asked for** — 3 clean isolated-preview runs show 7 of 8 pages now meaning 268–614ms TBT, both ends of that range below the old 440–1022ms figure, a real measured improvement. **Sharper finding, Cycle 86**: `calculator-bmi` — an eager, non-lazy "core hub" calculator, not part of the (a) fix — has a stable 1,360–2,184ms TBT across all 3 runs, and a CPU profile attributes ~84% of that to raw V8 script-parse/compile/GC time, not application logic. **Cycle 87 tested and ruled out the most obvious hypothesis for that time, with rigor, not a guess.** `Calculators.tsx` mounts every calculator-detail route in two full render passes: `selectedCalculator` starts `null` (placeholder), then a post-mount `useEffect` looks up the slug and sets it, forcing a second full render of the ~100-card selection grid + ~14-group chat-assisted section that stays mounted underneath every detail view. Fixed with a lazy `useState` initializer so a known slug resolves on the first render pass, eliminating the second pass entirely (`CALCULATORS` is a stable module-level array, so the effect's `setSelectedCalculator(match)` becomes a same-object-reference no-op on mount — verified via a full run of all 4 calculator test suites, 624/624 passing, `tsc`/eslint clean). **Re-measured with the identical 3-run + CPU-profile methodology: no improvement.** Pre-fix `calculator-bmi` TBT mean 1,674ms (1,359–2,184ms); post-fix mean 1,924ms (1,255–2,400ms) — same noisy range, no directional win. CPU profile before/after: 1,356ms and 1,494–1,996ms of `(program)` time respectively — unchanged within run-to-run variance. **The double-render was real and worth fixing on its own terms, but it was never the dominant cost** — a small fraction of ~1.5–2s of unattributed V8 time. That time is NOT explained by script-parse differences either: the calculator-hub route (263ms mean `(program)` time) loads nearly the identical script set (same `index`/`vendor`/`data-navigation` URLs, comparable per-URL self-time) as `calculator-bmi`/`sofa` (~1,350–1,650ms), yet is 5–6x faster. **Genuinely still unexplained**, narrowed rather than solved: the CPU-sampling profiler's `(program)`/`(no url)` bucket is a catch-all for VM-internal time it can't attribute to a JS frame, and this project's `scripts/perf-profile.mjs` only wraps `Profiler.stop()` (JS sampling) — it doesn't capture the CDP `Tracing` categories (Layout, Recalculate Style, Paint) that would distinguish "expensive render/layout" from "expensive script compile" as the real cause. That's the concrete next step, not another guess-and-measure pass. | Medium — two real hypotheses ruled out with before/after evidence (entry-chunk-splitting need unconfirmed, double-render confirmed-not-the-cause); a trace-based (not sampling-based) profile is the remaining path to a real root cause |
| P2 | Reduce the remaining inline-style violations toward zero | **Cycle 103: this row's own number was stale — re-measured fresh (`node scripts/check-inline-styles.mjs`) before touching anything, found 413/690 today, not the 451 on record (drifted in both directions across ~50 untracked cycles, net down). Fixed `QuickIntake.tsx`, the joint-highest offender (33 occurrences): classified all 33 by hand — 28 were static (theme-constant colors/sizes/layout, no state dependency) and 5 were genuinely dynamic (category-select/sex-select/priority-badge/priority-picker/submit-button, each keyed off component state). Moved the 28 static ones into a new `src/components/QuickIntake.css` (26 classes, same pattern as the reference `FullJourneyOperatingPage.css` migration), left the 5 dynamic ones inline untouched — matching this project's own established policy (`ALLOW_DYNAMIC` in the gate script) that genuinely state-dependent styling stays inline rather than forcing a CSS-variable workaround for a cosmetic-only win. Also deleted the now-dead `inputStyle` module constant and its `CSSProperties`/`MEDICAL_TYPE` imports once nothing referenced them, rather than leaving unused code behind.** Gate: **413→385/690**, the lowest verified total on record. Verified beyond the gate: frontend `tsc --noEmit` 0 errors, `eslint` 0 errors, the 3 test files that render this component (11/11 tests) plus the 6 service-level specs that exercise its call paths (30/30), and a full re-run of `routePagesSmoke.test.tsx` (258/258, includes jest-axe a11y coverage across route pages) — zero regressions, confirmed fresh, not assumed from the CSS being "just styling." **Cycle 104: closed the next same-shaped candidate, `StrokeCodeProtocol.tsx` (also 33 occurrences).** Same discipline: classified all 33 by hand — 27 static (theme-constant colors/sizes/layout, incl. the timeline's `grid-template-columns: repeat(${STROKE_TIMELINE_STEPS.length}, 1fr)`, safe to hardcode as `repeat(5, 1fr)` since that array is a fixed 5-element module constant, never runtime-variable) and 6 genuinely dynamic (button cursor/step-dot color/DTN-tone color/eligibility-tone color, each keyed off component state or a `toneColor()` call). Moved the 27 into a new `src/components/StrokeCodeProtocol.css` (20 classes), left the 6 dynamic ones inline. Gate: **385→358/690**. Verified: `tsc`/`eslint` 0/0, the component's own dedicated spec (4/4) plus 2 consumer specs that render it via `PatientDetailPanel` (8/8), and a full `routePagesSmoke.test.tsx` re-run (258/258). Still open: 358/690, and ~82 files still carry at least one occurrence — this two-file pass established the pattern is repeatable and low-risk, not that it's close to done. | Medium, needs browser QA per file |
| P2 | Decide backend `strictNullChecks: true` adoption | `backend/tsconfig.json` has `strict: true` immediately overridden by `strictNullChecks: false, noImplicitAny: false` — confirmed to degrade TS discriminated-union narrowing (root cause of 1 of the 4 tsc errors fixed this pass). Flipping it is exactly what a strict-TypeScript platform needs, but blast radius across ~800 backend files is unknown — needs its own baseline-then-fix pass, not a drive-by. | High |
